**2026-10-07 — SHADOW SCALPS: RANDOM-ENTRY CONTROL REMOVED (owner decision: "ok take it out then"). server/shadow-scalps.ts + shadow-scalps-routes.ts, ShadowScalpsCard.tsx, both shadow-scalps test suites (175/0 and 89/0), docs/shadow-scalps-README.md §0; tsc 0. Record-only as before; live after the owner's next restart.**
- WHAT CHANGED: (1) the recorder writes only S1 / S2 rows (nightly, catch-up, retry) — seededRng, the 20-draw hour-matched null generator, eligible-minute bookkeeping, Welch z and normalUpperP are deleted; (2) the first boot purges every `strategy LIKE 'null-%'` row (`purgeLegacyNullRows`, idempotent, chunks of 2,000 ids with a setImmediate between statements, ONE log line only when it removed rows, retried next boot on a throw); (3) the route body lost nullNetPerTrade / nullN / minusNull / z / p (rows) and nullNetPerTrade / z / p / promotion.beatsNullOk (decisions) and rules.promoteAlpha; reads filter `strategy IN ('S1','S2')` in SQL and the pure summary skips other strategies, so leftover control rows are invisible before the purge; (4) the digest line never named the control — unchanged; (5) the card lost the RANDOM and Δ vs RANDOM columns (9-column grid, header now an exported SHADOW_HEADER array) and the "Random rows are the control" sentence; footnote = "Record only — no orders, no alerts."
- NEW PROMOTION RULE (era=live, pessimistic fills, 1.0-pt friction, decision cell TP6/SL8): KILL when live n >= 100 and net/trade < -0.3 (unchanged); PROMOTABLE when live n >= 300, net/trade >= +0.3, both halves > 0, and net > 0 with the best 3 session days removed. `promotion` = exactly {nOk, netOk, halvesOk, dropBest3Ok, all}.
- WITHOUT A CONTROL THE BAR IS THE BRACKET'S BREAK-EVEN ARITHMETIC plus the robustness checks: 6/8 at 1.0 pt = +5 / -9 net -> 9/14 = 64.3 % wins to break even (64.9 % with the pessimistic 1-tick stop slip, 9.25/14.25); +0.3/trade needs ~67.0 % wins at pessimistic fills. Pinned by a test: 64 % -> -0.13/tr, 65 % -> +0.01, 67 % -> +0.2975 (fails), 68 % -> +0.44 (passes). Halves + drop-best-3 guard against a lucky stretch but cannot separate an edge from tape any entry would have caught — the manual block-bootstrap Apex check and 50+ SIM hand-trades after promotion stay.
- LIVE TABLE TODAY (read-only count of data/app.db): 25,920 control rows (null-S1 16,020 / 89 days, null-S2 9,900 / 55 days) + 5,443 strategy rows (S1 873, S2 4,570), all era backfill. LIVE PURGE DONE: the server restarted externally at 11:42 ET (no shutdown line, not caused here) and the boot logged "removed 25920 legacy random-control rows ... max chunk 149ms, 2165ms total" (slower than offline: boot catch-up + gap audit + chart reads on the same SQLite file); the table now holds only the 5,443 S1/S2 rows and the live route body has no null/z/p keys. Side effect: sessions now count only days with an S1/S2 row (87, was 89), so the halves split moved 08-04 -> 08-05 and the S1 backfill halves read -0.17 / +0.25 (were -0.28 / +0.34); totals unchanged. Measured on a temp table of 32,040 control + 3,204 strategy rows: chunked purge 116-125 ms total, max chunk 33-34 ms (event-loop max delay 33-35 ms); one DELETE was 67-94 ms in a single block. Second run: 0 rows, 0 ms, no log.

**2026-10-07 — MISSED-DAY CATCH-UP: whole sessions the computer was off for are now back-filled (BF badge) once their 1m bars are healed (server/catchup.ts bottom section, scheduler tick step 0 + digest line, SignalsView BF tooltip; scripts/catchup-missed-days.test.ts 66/0 in npm test; docs/catchup-missed-days-README.md; tsc 0)**
- OWNER ASK: "if i turn off my computer, do you still create signals … i want this so i can see if the program is still running correctly". ANSWER BEFORE THIS: no. The 10-min catch-up replayed 90 days but kept only fires whose sessionDayKey === today, so every fully-off session stayed empty forever (the Sunday regen that used to re-baseline them is dormant).
- BUILD: (1) detection = Mon–Fri Globex sessions (CME full closures skipped via `CME_EQUITY_SPECIAL_SESSIONS`, 2026–2027, early halts 13:00/13:15/10:15 confirmed against the store's 1135–1140 / 953 bar counts) between the coverage ANCHOR (the last good today pass; first use seeds it from scheduler_last_catchup only if ok, else now, so a fresh install invents nothing) and today. The anchor's own day counts only if that pass ran > 15 min before the session close (the 10-min cadence always leaves the last minutes). Recoverable = the 7 session days before today (`MAX_MISSED_SESSIONS`); older days are REPORTED as unrecoverable, never faked. (2) per day, oldest first: the replay with priorFires widened to session start − 3 d (`loadPriorFires(now, sym, fromTs)` / `BuildContextOptions.priorFromTs`), only that day's fires kept, the SAME diff + admitFires + guarded POST + source='catchup' as the today pass (extracted VERBATIM into `backfillSessionFires`; the today pass's output is unchanged). (3) a day runs only at ≥ 90 % 1m coverage of its session minutes; otherwise it is deferred "bars not healed yet". Strict order: later days wait 6 attempts (~1 h) for an unhealed older day, then go ahead. Aged-out pending days become "bars never healed (x%)". (4) scheduler step 0: the first tick after the 3-min boot quiet sweeps every ready day (20 s apart) BEFORE the today pass (chronological), at any hour (a Saturday boot fills Thu/Fri); afterwards ≤ 1 day per 10 min. (5) digest: "Catch-up: back-filled N missed session day(s): <dates> (M fires); K day(s) beyond the 7-day bar depth not recoverable (<dates>); W day(s) waiting for healed 1m bars (<date> x%) ⚠️" (24 h window, absent when idle). (6) BF tooltip shows the Globex session date (`fmtEtDate(ts + 6 h)`) and "or the computer was off".
- RULE (worker channel): the worker's `{type:"catchup"}` message carries no argument and live-engine*.ts were out of scope, so the day travels in app_settings `catchup_missed_job` {day, at}, written right before the delegate call and cleared in finally. computeCatchup reads it (15-min TTL). runCatchupPass clears any leftover job BEFORE its compute and throws on an answer carrying `missedDay` (a stale job can never make a today pass diff the wrong day). All state writes happen under the catch-up `running` flag; the scheduler only calls `missedDaysPendingCount()` while no pass runs.
- RULE (main-thread cost): a missed-day compute costs what a today compute costs (~13 s worker CPU + the served-window fetches) and holds `running`, during which live-engine skips its 10 s tick. That is why the boot sweep releases the flag for 20 s between days and why the 10-min step does only one day.
- STATE: app_settings `catchup_missed_days` {anchorAt, pending, deferred, done[40], unrecoverable[40], lastError}. Re-running a day is idempotent (only absent keys are posted), so to force a re-run put the date back in pending and remove it from done.
- KNOWN, NOT CHANGED: (a) back-filled rows older than the 2-day edge land in integrity-check V1 "regen row not in book", the class that already exists for every catch-up row while the regen is dormant. (b) lateEntryScan (live-engine.ts, not this session's file) considers ANY open row ≤ 6 h old regardless of source. A boot shortly after an evening outage could therefore late-enter a missed-day row, exactly as it can a today catch-up row. Only when armed + lateEntryEnabled. (c) Not retroactive: detection starts from the last good pass before the restart that loads this code. (d) The holiday table needs a yearly extension; an unlisted holiday only waits and ages out as reported, it is never faked.
- VERIFIED: tsc 0; new suite 66/0. Six mutants (no coverage gate, no chronological wait, no prior widening, no tail grace, no job clear, no day filter) each fail it. Every npm-test suite was run individually: green except the tolerated fact-engine 364/2 and fact-engine-parity 11/1. Live server NOT restarted (the feature is live after the owner's next restart); data/app.db only read (read-only probes); trade settings untouched; no git.

**2026-10-07 — MORNING CANDLE-PATTERN SCALPS: adversarial refute of C:/BaxterSandbox/analysis/morning-scalps (owner: "3-5 pt morning scalps with per-candle pattern recognition") — the "no" HOLDS; refute scripts in C:/BaxterSandbox/analysis/morning-scalps/refute/**
efute\**
- Independent pure-Python bar-by-bar recompute (recompute.py, grid_placebo.py, midbar_entry.py; strict same-bar TP+SL = loss) reproduced every claimed cell to the decimal: top-3 pess F1.0 cells inside/vec+yb/short 5/8 n54 +0.62 (H1 +0.86 / H2 +0.37, -6.50 without best 3 of 27 days), 6/8 n50 +0.49 (-20.75), mom2/after1030/short 6/8 n49 +0.39 (-6.75); owner's 3/3 4/4 5/5 cells -0.83..-1.27 std / -0.96..-1.57 pess per trade at 46-52 % win vs random control 44-50 %; full grid at pess F1.0 = 11 of 636 positive, 5 both-halves, best +0.62 (identical counts to the original); walk-forward 5 of 6 families negative in H2.
- NO LOOKAHEAD: truncation test 400/400 identical; frozen box = PRIOR trading day's 17:00 ET close ± 50-day 60m-extension mean (0 of 67 boxes match the same-day close); vector inclusive of the signal bar only. Same-bar ambiguity is immaterial (0-1 ambiguous exit bars in the top cells; the optimistic/standard spread <= 0.3/trade).
- NOT SIGNIFICANT even before multiple-comparison correction: per-day sign-flip p = 0.20 / 0.27 / 0.32 for the top 3; 200-iteration placebo with the same per-session signal counts gives mean 2.8 both-halves-positive cells (p95 7, max 11), 19 % of placebos >= the real 5, and 64.5 % of placebo grids have a best cell >= +0.62 (placebo best mean +0.80).
- HAND-TRADER LATENCY KILLS THE INSIDE CELLS: the inside/vec+yb/short edge lives in the first seconds of the entry bar (fills drift 0.18 pt worse by mid-bar, 0.32 by its close): 5/8 goes +0.62 -> +0.25 (mid, H2 -0.45) -> -0.01 (close) -> -0.58 (one bar late); 6/8 +0.49 -> +0.17 -> -0.11 -> -0.35. mom2/after1030/short is latency-robust (+0.39 open, +0.43 mid, +0.70 one bar late; the entry bar pulls back 0.18-0.30 pt in the short's favour) but trades on 36 days, n49, p 0.32 and is inside the placebo range. Nothing to ship; LEARNINGS 2026-10-06 scalping conclusion stands.

**2026-10-06 — SHADOW SCALPS RECORDER SHIPPED (server/shadow-scalps.ts + shadow-scalps-routes.ts, shared/schema.ts shadow_scalps table, db.ts DDL, scheduler tick step 5 + digest line, index.ts registration; scripts/shadow-scalps.test.ts 165/0 in the test chain; tsc 0; docs/shadow-scalps-README.md). Record-only — no orders, no trade-settings change; live after the owner's restart (it already ran once on the 07:08 ET 10-07 boot: 89 sessions / 31,363 rows, max chunk 317 ms).**
- WHAT: S1 ORB-30 (09:30–09:59 range, first 1m CLOSE beyond, 1 Long + 1 Short, signal bar before 12:00 ET) and S2 yellow-box edge fade ([limit] resting-limit and [close] close-back-inside variants; day box = server/yellowbox.ts getYellowboxDayZones frozen row), each walked through cells 6/8 (decision) + 4/6 and 5/8 (learning) × optimistic/standard/pessimistic fills, plus 20 seeded hour-matched null draws per session per strategy. Flat at 16:55 = close of the 16:54 bar. Unique key symbol|day|strategy|variant|direction|signal_ts|seq|cell|fill_model; a day is DELETE+INSERT idempotent and keeps its era.
- ERAS: empty table → last 90 finished sessions as era=backfill (preview only, NEVER counts for kill/promotion); afterwards boot catch-up + nightly weekdays ≥17:30 ET once per date as era=live. Kill = live n≥100 & net < −0.3 (pessimistic, 1.0 friction); promotion = live n≥300, net ≥ +0.3, both halves > 0, beats the null at one-sided p < 0.05/3 (Welch z), survives drop-best-3 — the block-bootstrap Apex check + 50 SIM hand-trades stay manual.
- BACKFILL PREVIEW (era=backfill, pess 6/8, 1.0): S1 n=97 +0.05/tr (H −0.28/+0.34, null −1.38, z 2.01); S2 limit n=279 −1.73 (would already be past the kill line if live); S2 close n=167 −0.89. Agrees with the 10-06 research: nothing to trade, S1 is the only family worth a live count.
- MAIN-THREAD LESSONS (two review rounds): (1) a per-session synchronous chunk that calls the yellowbox source COLD (bar load + mwml registry + INSERT) was 328 ms median / up to 1.9 s per chunk — fixed by a cache-only SQLite handle (`cacheOnlySqlite`: only `SELECT … FROM yellowbox_day_zones` and the 1-row cached_candles probe prepare; anything else throws → day recorded with S2 pending, retried ≤10 days per 10-min pass once a current-ver cache row exists). Cold-cache boot: 89 sessions in ~1 s, max chunk 74 ms, 0 yellowbox writes. (2) The summary route and digest were a single synchronous read+summarize (~200 ms on 31k rows) — now keyset-paged 4,000 rows per turn + an 8 ms-slice generator; max block 27–30 ms. (3) Under boot contention a warm chunk still measured 317 ms live (busy_timeout inside the DELETE+INSERT while shadow-exits/catch-up wrote) — the write commit is the irreducible part; everything else is < 11 ms.
- OPEN (documented in README §3): the retry pre-check only tests `ver === YB_CACHE_VER`, so a pending day with a current-ver but stale-hash cache row is re-recorded every 10 min (~10–20 ms, one log line) until a chart/catch-up GET refreshes the row — self-resolving, cheap fix = compare import_hash + traded=1 in the pre-check. The first frozen-box read per process can still cost 415–585 ms once (registry load) when no chart has loaded day-zones first. The ShadowScalpsCard (client, SignalsView) was built by another session and is outside this entry.
- RULES: eras are the firewall — a preview computed from the past must never be able to satisfy a forward rule (schema-level `era` + SQL filter, not a convention); every job that touches the DB from the scheduler gets its own cache-only / read-only handle so a "warm path" cannot silently become a cold recompute; measure event-loop delay (`monitorEventLoopDelay`) per chunk, not wall time, and record the LIVE boot number in the doc next to the offline one.

The Extensions jar (19,584 B, 2026-08-14) is a build of the regressed source. INGEST CLEARED: the study emits `levels:[{price,b,a}]`, `live-bars.ts` passes b/a through untouched and `footprint-engine.addBar` maps b→bidVol, a→askVol (the new test proves a two-sided message lands level-for-level). ENGINE CLEARED: `shared/fact-engine.ts` reads a buy stack as Long support and a sell stack as Short resistance — correct for real data; the Short-only output came from `footprint-engine.closeCandle()`'s zero-vs-nonzero rule: with a dead ask side EVERY level is a "sell" imbalance, so each bar collapses into ONE stacked sell cluster spanning its whole range (test reproduces it: 5 levels → 1 stacked sell cluster 7702→7703) → a Short footprint fact on roughly every bar closing in its lower half and never a Long (37/37 footprint fires Short since July, 4,539 sell stacks vs 0 buy — scalping doc §9).

Compile recipe TODAY: JDK 25 (`C:\Program Files\Eclipse Adoptium\jdk-25.0.3.9-hotspot`) against the 09-18 SDK jar, which is class-file v69 — the 2026-08-04 byte-patch (70→69) recipe is OBSOLETE, javac reads it directly. NOT INSTALLED: MotiveWave untouched (AutoTrader armed; a MW restart deactivates strategies and drops the feed — the owner's call). **2026-10-06 — SCALPING RESEARCH (docs/scalping-research-2026-10-06.md): 3–5 pt targets every few candles do NOT add up for this trader; nothing survives realistic fills**
- FRICTION MATH: MES round trip ≈ 0.7 / 1.0 / 1.5 pt (good RTH day / project standard / open-news-overnight) = 23–50 % of a 3-pt target. Break-even win rate (SL+F)/(TP+SL) vs the RANDOM-ENTRY hit rate on our own RTH 1m bars: 4/6 needs 70 % at 1.0 pt, random gets 59.7 %; 3/3 needs 66.7 %, random 49.3 %; 4/23 needs 88.9 %, random 80.5 % (a high win rate is not an edge). Random entries net −0.94 to −1.31/trade in EVERY cell; "every few candles" with no edge loses 19–79 pts/day per contract.
- 19 scalp entry variants tested on 3 months (engine fires re-scored 2–6 × 3–12; yellow-box edge fade and break-retest; VWAP 1σ/2σ fades; ORB-15/30; vector pullback; first pullback; dead-tape range scalp): NONE positive in both halves under pessimistic fills (target needs a 1-tick trade-through, stop fills 1 tick worse — the Rithmic tick proxy says only 53–62 % of exact touches fill). Engine fires hit small targets at exactly the random rate (0 of 3,888 cells net-positive). The yellow-box edge fade has REAL direction skill (t vs null 1.6–3.0) worth only +0.5–0.63 gross vs 0.7–1.0 cost → −0.4 to −0.6 net; a one-bar-late entry erases it.
- Apex: no minimum hold time, but bans HFT, non-directional brackets, automation and "small profit targets while risking disproportionately large amounts" (its example 5 vs 150 ticks — 4/23 is 16 vs 92): the stop must shrink with the target. Commission $0.51/side MES (+ ~$0.72 RT exchange/NFA).
- SHADOW (record-only, no orders) only: S1 ORB-30 (first 1m close beyond the 09:30–10:00 range, ≤1/side/day, decision cell TP6/SL8 — the only family with positive cells, H2 only, t<1); S2 yellow-box edge fade with a RESTING LIMIT entry on tick data (the only lever that cuts friction). Kill at n≥100 if net < −0.3; promotion needs n≥300, ≥+0.3 net at pessimistic fills, both halves positive, beats hour-matched random with multiple-testing correction, survives drop-best-3, block-bootstrap Apex check.

**2026-10-01 (late) — TICKETS 12a/12b VERIFIED AND FIXED (docs/signal-analysis-2026-10-01.md §6.12): the admission one-open check looked backward only, and the FORMING 5m/15m/60m bucket that always sits in cached_candles was both walked around (coarse-bar outcomes) and fired on (partial-bar entries)**
- 12a REAL, narrow. `admitFires` (server/fire-admission.ts) checked one-open-per-direction only against EARLIER stored rows. The engine's priors seed cannot block bars BEFORE a stored fire (no lookahead, by design), so a catch-up/tab replay that lands the same idea 11+ bars AHEAD of an ordered live fire — outside the cooldown, same target, its own walk still open at the stored fire's entry — was admitted: two or more open same-direction positions on one interval against one target. FIX: the stored-row query reads OPEN_LOOKBACK_SEC on BOTH sides and a candidate is refused ("open", blockedBy = the later key) when its own record is still open at a LATER same-direction stored row's entry; the stored (ordered) row is the real trade, the earlier replay twin yields. Reproduced + covered in scripts/fire-admission.test.ts B1+ (two twins at T−24/T−12 bars, both admitted pre-fix → both refused; 67/0; the pre-fix module fails 4 of them). DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED: a same-target re-fire AFTER the stored trade closed is sequential by the engine's own rule (cooldown + one open) — not stacking; changing it is an R-rule (§5), so it is pinned as admitted.
- 12b REAL, two halves, one root cause. server/derive-bars.ts rewrites the 5m/15m/60m bucket containing every just-completed 1m bar, so cached_candles ALWAYS holds the forming bucket (read-only check 16:18 ET: res 5 and 15 max = 20:15Z, res 60 = 20:00Z, at 20:18Z) and cached-continuous serves it as a plain candle — only the browser tab's WS merge carries complete:false, and market.tsx (~L1497) already drops DB bars whose close > now; the server writers never did. (i) WALK: walkForward/bracketExitTs required the 1m slice to reach the primary's LAST bar's end; a forming bucket's end is in the future, so the 1m "never covered" and every 5m/15m/60m walk in the catch-up (always) and in late/retried live passes fell to the coarse bars — SL-first inside one bar spanning both levels, the 2026-07-31 false-record class through a side door, first-touch-LOCKED as loss (resolveStuckRows walks only open rows, so it can never repair it). (ii) FIRING: the bar-close loop skipped only complete:false bars, so the forming bucket was evaluated as a CLOSED bar at its open time on partial OHLC. Read-only audit of the last 30 days: 20 of 354 stored 5m/15m/60m rows carry an entry that is not their bar's final close, up to 22.5 pts (09-28 17:00Z 15m Long entry 7757.25 vs close 7779.75, recorded win_tp1 two minutes after the bar closed = a partial-bar entry whose target the bar's own continuation crossed; 10-01 18:00Z 15m catch-up Short 7710.25 vs 7717.75). A partial-bar row also pre-empts the real bar-close fire of the same key (live engine DB-absence check → "already persisted", no order) and lateEntryScan can order it.
- FIX (shared/fact-engine.ts): `closedEdgeTs` — the newest primary bar CLOSED by nowSec — is the 1m coverage edge for both walk sites; the bar-close loop skips any bar whose close > nowSec (every real caller passes nowSec = now, so closed bars are untouched — fixture clocks that sat inside a bar were corrected); shared/outcome-resolver.ts never walks a complete:false bar; evaluateFormingBar's one-open gate keeps its documented "extremes so far" by appending the forming bar without the flag. Covered by scripts/forming-bar-walk.test.ts (24/0, added to npm test): pre-fix engine → the forming bucket turns the 1m win_tp1 into a coarse loss AND fires a second signal at the bucket's open time; pre-fix resolver fails 3. resolveStuckRows was already right (1m walk, newest served bar excluded) — pinned there too.
- VERIFIED: tsc 0; every suite run individually is green except the 2 tolerated fact-engine 'generated config' failures and the pre-existing fact-engine-parity 11/1 (DB-vs-standing-JSON drift, see the evening entry above). Live server NOT restarted (plain tsx, no watcher — the fix is live only after the owner's next restart; the tab halves on reload); DB opened read-only; trade settings untouched. Edits were made in the MAIN checkout (uncommitted, like the rest of this round); copies of the pre-edit files sit in C:\Users\Jackson\day-trading-backups\20261001-162518-000.
- NOT DONE (own session + backup, like §6.2): re-resolve the rows the coarse walk locked and purge/annotate the partial-bar-entry rows (the 20 above — the 09-28 17:00Z +10.5 'win' is not a real record). Ticket 12b's "5 of 79 60m outcomes flip" in the replay is this mechanism.
- RULE: `npm test` is an && chain — the FIRST suite (fact-engine.test.ts) exits 1 on the two tolerated failures, so nothing after it runs. Run the suites individually (per-suite loop) until those two are fixed or the chain tolerates them; a green `npm test` tail today proves only the first suite.
- RULE: any candle array that comes from cached_candles may END with a forming bucket. "Closed" is a close time ≤ now (or an explicit complete flag), never a position in the array — on every path (firing, walk coverage, record walks).
- RULE (session mechanics): the Bash tool truncates commands past ~10 KB (heredocs die with "unexpected EOF while looking for matching `''"); stage long content through a scratchpad file and copy/insert it with a short command. The Write tool is hook-blocked on the main checkout from a worktree session; shell writes are not, so mirror the edit-backup hook by hand (copy to day-trading-backups first).

**2026-10-01 (late) — SHADOW RULES R1–R4 + SET B BUILT OFF-BY-DEFAULT, SHADOW TAGS RECORDED ON EVERY FIRE (C:\BaxterSandbox\analysis\shadow-rules\SUMMARY.md)**
- ENGINE (shared/fact-engine.ts): REQUIRE_BOX_SIDE (false), MIN_SESSION_RANGE_FRAC / MAX_SESSION_RANGE_FRAC (0 = off), BLOCK_TIGHT_ROOM_INTERVALS ([]), MIN_TP1_PTS_BY_INTERVAL ({}). One predicate per rule (`isBoxSideWrong`, `sessionRangeRatio`, the `tight-room` risk flag, `ExitResult.nearestDist`) feeds BOTH the setting (`shadowRuleBlock`, `exitWithIntervalFloor`) and the record-only tag (`computeShadowTags`), so a tag means exactly "this setting would have blocked this fire". Blocks are silent (no cooldown, no open-trade state), sit after the exit geometry in the bar-close loop and before the return in evaluateFormingBar, and are counted in `statsOut.shadowRuleSuppressed` (a lazy key, so default runs are byte-identical). No day zone / no median = not judged (no block, no tag).
- RULE: computeRiskFlags now runs BEFORE the walk (it is pure). BLOCK_TIGHT_ROOM_INTERVALS reads it; with the defaults nothing reads it, and the golden fire set is unchanged (1m fb9c8132 / 5m 5a510da1).
- PERSIST: signal_history.shadow_tags TEXT (JSON array; '[]' = evaluated, nothing tripped; NULL = pre-feature). writeSignalRows normalizes array or string input and COALESCE-guards conflict writes. The POST route, catch-up and live-engine rows pass `shadowTags` through. The market.tsx tab does NOT send them yet (not this session's file), and fe-bt --persist regen rows stay NULL. Scoring: GET /api/signals/shadow-tags/summary?days=90. It de-clusters with a one-open-per-direction walk per interval over every window row, applies −1 pt friction, and marks judgeable at ≥40 de-clustered tagged fires.
- HARNESS: fe-bt `--require-box-side --min-session-range-frac x --max-session-range-frac x --block-tight-room 5m,15m --min-tp1-by-interval 1m=12.25`. These flags run in window mode only, refuse --persist, write `.shadow-rules` outputs and imply `--strict-readonly`. `--strict-readonly` is new: the DB is opened readonly:true with no read-write fallback. CSV gains a trailing `shadowTags` column. A full window replay takes ~10 s; `scripts/analysis/shadow-rules-replay.ts` runs every variant.
- CHAIN-AWARE REPLAY (06-24→10-01, shipped gate, −1 friction, IN-SAMPLE thresholds): baseline 617 / −1.43 (H1 −1.14 / H2 −1.79). R1 465 / −1.17 (H1 worse: −1.40). R2 506 / −0.72 (H2 −0.17). **2026-10-01 — PER-SIGNAL LINE-UP ANALYSIS (docs/signal-analysis-2026-10-01.md; dataset of 883 ungated / 609 gated engine fires 06-24→10-01 with every strategy's status + 1m path, independently verified; 4 lenses, 10 refuters)**
- WHY THE BOOK IS NEGATIVE: (1) the Signals tab's last 30 days (−3,117 pts, n=1,530) were ~87 % stacking under the rules retired 09-24 (09-04 alone: 91 stacked 1m Longs, 4.4 % win, −2,208); re-walked under current rules the same 30 days are n=213, −389. (2) Under CURRENT rules the 3-month gated replay still loses: n=609, 71.4 % win, −1.09/trade, PF 0.85 (H1 −0.86 / H2 −1.38; day-block 90 % CI [−2.32, +0.03]). Bracket arithmetic on the wrong fires: avg win +9.7 vs avg loss −24.5 → needs ~74.5 % wins, gets 71.4. ETH carries 96 % of the gated loss. Tabletop-anchored 1m targets (median TP 5.75 vs SL 27.25) = −373 of −664.
- WHAT LINES UP: NOTHING robustly. 0 of 254 single strategy statuses pass both-halves + CI>0; pairs/triples pass at the rate of random shuffles. 'More agreement' is WORSE (4/4 vectors agreeing −0.97 vs 2/4 +0.93; confidence-95 fires are the worst band). Only robust NEGATIVE statuses: fire INSIDE the yellow box or on its wrong side (ungated n=266 −3.13 PF 0.64, both halves negative), and session range so far < 0.25× the dead-tape median (n=149 −4.49, mostly the 18:00–21:00 Globex reopen). Combos positive in both halves: FG+Fr+Vec@1m (n=35 +2.07 — the 09-25 sandbox re-gate would BLOCK it; hand-check before accepting any regen) and Fr+YB@60m (n=26 +1.78); worst: Fr+YB@1m (n=90 −2.62).
- REFUTED, do not act on: the 15m-slope-opposes block (family-wise p 0.43–0.89 after 2,000 permutations), 15m+60m oppose, 'de-clustered live book confirms the replay', the RTH/ETH half 'regime flip' (ETH never won a half), 'the 1m default bracket is net positive' (t 0.60). The avgWin/avgLoss break-even 'diagnosis' is an algebraic identity, not a cause.
- EXITS (again): no breakeven move (−1.20/−1.08/−1.10 vs −1.11 shipped), no global stop cap, 15m stays 10.5/26. The post-entry path is SYMMETRIC (median MFE = median MAE at every horizon) so no exit can create an edge; the best anchor-aware policy reaches +0.06/trade and halves max DD (838→461) — stops the bleed, no edge.
- SHADOW CANDIDATES (approval pending; chain effect of R1–R3 unknown until a sandbox fe-bt replay): R1 REQUIRE_BOX_SIDE (Long only above the box, Short only below, never inside); R2 MIN_SESSION_RANGE_FRAC 0.25 (no fire until the session range ≥ 0.25× median); R3 tight-room block on 5m/15m (tiny n); R4 1m MIN_TP1_PTS 12.25 (chain-aware re-run: 358 fires −0.87 → 236 fires +0.60, best of 3 floors = in-sample); R5 cross-interval one-open-per-direction (halves exposure, caps the −1,049 all-stop clusters; per-trade edge unchanged). Set B (R1+R2+range<1× median+no tight-room): gated n=297 +0.95 PF 1.16 but chosen with both halves visible.
- METHOD RULES: judge the system only on session days from 2026-09-27 (current rules) and report older rows separately; every status test needs a family-wise null with ≥2,000 permutations (40 was noise); row-removal tests ignore the cooldown/open-trade chain — only engine re-runs count; ~500 effective trades → per-trade SE ≈ 0.65 pts, so +0.5..+1.0 results are ≤1.5 SE from zero.
- DATA DEFECTS FOUND: the 09-14 roll seam (+67 pts) contaminates 38 rows' crossing cells; a 09-04 phantom bar; 10 stored rows whose stored LOSS is a WIN on the served bars (+371 pts, re-resolve with a backup); catch-up can stack same-direction fires against one unchanged target; whether HTF outcomes are walked on coarse bars because the forming HTF bar sits in the DB is unverified.

**2026-10-01 — VERIFIER ROUND on the owner-approved settings (R1 RTH-only orders, R2a muted overnight alerts, R3 news blackout, Apex 25K): calendar staleness alarm, the $1,500 that was a TARGET, losses that must carry overnight**
- APEX 25K: the owner said "25000 with a 1500 target". $1,500 is the PROFIT TARGET. Current Apex 25K plans trail a $1,000 drawdown; only the retired legacy 25K had a $1,500 threshold. The first round shipped headroom 1500/margin 500, which under a current plan pauses at exactly $1,000 used, the liquidation point. Default is now 1000/500 (pause at $500 used = 100 pts at 1 MES, 50 at 2), safe under both plans. The owner sets 1500 only if RTrader shows a legacy threshold.

Fix: `apexGuardProfile` tag ("apex-25k-2026-10-01"). A stored row from another profile (every pre-25K row has none) hydrates everything EXCEPT the guard dollars, with a loud boot log. The next settings POST stamps the tag, and from then on the owner's values stick. Bump the tag on the next account switch.
- ACCOUNT CARRY-OVER: the guard tracker zeroed at 09:25 every day while Apex's threshold is lifetime-anchored (evaluations never lock). A −$1,040 day was followed by a fresh $1,000 allowance. The guard now also keeps an ACCOUNT tracker (`acctRealized/acctPeak/acctSince`) that does not roll. It is seeded from the stored day on upgrade, so today's −$1,040 carries, and only "Reset guard tracker" (POST /api/trade/guard/reset) re-anchors it. `apexGuardCarryOver:false` restores the per-day tracker. New `apexDailyLossLimitDollars` (0 = off) pauses orders when the RTH day's net including open losses reaches −$N; for an EOD 25K (DLL $500) set it to about 300.
- NEWS CALENDAR: no test opened the real data/news-calendar.json, and the server fails OPEN without it. New `scripts/news-calendar-data.test.ts` loads the real file and checks weekday dates, known print times, monthly CPI/jobs/PCE, rules copy == code literal, window.to >= today, and a print in the next 30 days. That makes it a DELIBERATE time bomb that goes red when the refresh is due. The digest also prints a "News calendar:" line (warns 14 d before window.to), and integrity-check has a new N1 warning (`newsCalendarHealth`).
- `window` = COMPLETE coverage. window.to was 2027-01-31 while 8 of 9 January prints were unpublished, so /api/news/blackouts said January was covered. It is now 2026-12-31, with `partialThrough` 2027-01-31 for the FOMC Jan 27 row. `calendarCovers` also honours window.from. The file's `blackoutRules` are INFORMATIONAL: `BLACKOUT_RULES` in news-blackout.ts governs, and drift is flagged. ISM dates are plausible but NOT independently verified (the site 302s scripted fetches to SSO). A claimed "CPI Jan 13 2027" was the 2026 row; bls.gov has no 2027 rows yet.

The server sends no push for engine fires; the only server-side fire alert is POST /api/discord/send, which is muted. trade-notify covers order events only, and the discord-reader push (third-party signals, default off) is not gated.
