**2026-10-01 — OVERNIGHT (ETH) RESEARCH + INTERNAL OVERNIGHT AUDIT (docs/eth-trading-research-2026-10-01.md; 12/12 academic claims fact-checked against their sources)**
- RULE: the overnight loss lives in ENTRY SELECTION, not the bracket. The ETH sleeve is negative in every population (live book 3 mo n=3,635 −4,006 pts; de-clustered −136; current-rules gated replay n=453 −750 = −1.66/trade PF 0.78); ~97 % of the live-book loss was the clustering fixed 2026-09-24, the rest is structural: median 1m overnight bracket TP 12.25 / SL 25 needs 69.8 % wins after friction, the sleeve wins 68–72 %. The overnight tape is SLOWER not lopsided (favourable vs adverse 30-min moves 8.25 vs 7.75) — no exit can create an edge there (exit MC: 12,232 variants, none survives held-out).
- A 09:29 (or 08:25) forced flat is MEASURED WORSE (1m −2,971 → −4,946): about a third of overnight trades recover after the open. Overnight hour profiles are regime-unstable (worst hours flip between books and months; 60m-vector alignment flips sign) — do NOT gate by hour or by 60m alignment; the 2026-08-20 04:00–07:30 window study has flipped.
- Published evidence (NY Fed SR 917; Liberty Street 2026-07-01): the only robust ES overnight drift was 02:00–03:00 ET and it is gone since 2021 (and negative after spread). Asia hours carry ~1/50 of US volume; overnight hourly sigma ≈ 0.45 × RTH (our bars: 0.47). 8:30/10:00 ET releases are jump events with ~40 % of the move as informed drift 30 min BEFORE (our 8:30 bar p90 20.75 pts ≈ a stop) → a scheduled-news entry blackout is the one overnight-adjacent rule with evidence behind it.
- Apex: trading day 18:00–16:59 ET, the daily loss limit resets at 18:00 and counts open losses, a DLL hit pauses the REST of that session (an overnight loss spends the RTH day) — hand-trade RTH only. Pending user approvals R1–R6 in the report (RTH-only, mute overnight confluence alerts or run the ETH_CONFLUENCE:false replay, news blackout, ETH_MIN_AGREEING_FACTS=3 shadow test, hour-scaled shadow stop, plan-type check + $250/night cap).

**2026-10-01 — EXIT-STRATEGY SWEEP + MONTE CARLO on the 3-month fires (scripts/analysis/exit-monte-carlo.ts, outputs C:\BaxterSandbox\analysis\exit-mc\ — analysis only, nothing shipped)**
- METHOD: 978 fires (signals-3mo.json) x 12,232 single-exit variants (TP x SL grid 6..30, breakeven +4..12, time stop 15..240 min, MFE-giveback trail m6..20/g3..10, ATR14 multiples, shipped) scored carry AND Apex flat-16:55, one-open-per-direction re-applied per variant, NET = gross - 1. The dataset's 12 sampled crossing levels cannot resolve a 1-pt grid/BE/trail, so every path is re-walked from loadData 1m bars; the shipped exit re-walk matched the dataset's canonical outcomes 978/978 (carry) and 978/978 (Apex) before any variant was trusted. Moved stops (BE/trail) fill at the bar OPEN when it gaps through them.
- RESULT: exits do not cure the bleed, they stop it. Walk-forward (best on H1 with bootstrap P95 DD <= 300, scored on H2) moves gated 1m Apex from H2 -1.46/tr to ~0 (be+9 TP10/SL11 +0.05, trail m18/g3 SL10 0.00) and P(Apex blown in 60 days) from 34% to <4%, but nothing at 1m is positive held-out. Positive held-out under Apex: 5m trail m18/g3 SL12 (H1 +0.88 / H2 +0.84, a neighbour plateau), 60m time240m TP30/SL18 (H2 +0.31 vs shipped -2.01), 15m time240m TP30/SL22 (H2 +0.68, n=19 only). Pattern: wide target + 3-4 h time stop helps at 15m/60m; ATR/breakeven families overfit H1 and fail H2.
- RULE: an "exit fix" for a sleeve whose ungated MFE/MAE profile is ~symmetric can only reach breakeven — entry selection, not the bracket, is where the 1m loss lives.

**2026-09-25 — SANDBOXED 3-MONTH RE-RUN ON THE NEW ENGINE RULES (cooldown 10, YB break = 3-bar event, one open per direction): fe-bt in C:BaxterSandboxe-bt-20260924 (working-tree copy outside OneDrive, data/ junction opened READ-ONLY, BAXTER_ARTIFACTS_DIR → sandbox, WINDOW_START_KEY rolled to 2026-06-24, NO --persist) — the live gate/book untouched, awaiting the user's accept/reject.**
- RESULT (window 2026-06-24→09-25, NET after 1 pt friction): BEFORE (old engine + old gate/exits) 786 trades, −1.15/trade, −906 pts, PF 0.84 → AFTER (new rules + re-derived gate + MC exits) 347 trades, +0.29/trade, +99 pts, PF 1.04. By class: fact-engine@1m n=51 68.6% +0.93/tr (+47); vse@1m n=135 +0.41/tr (+56); @60m n=69 +1.48/tr (+102); @5m n=41 −0.50/tr (−20); @15m n=24 −0.58/tr (−14, POOLED calibration TP 22/SL 21.75 — would overwrite the user-approved 10.5/26 hand calibration: NEVER accept the 15m row, re-apply scripts/bestprogram-gate-patch.ts after any persist); vse@15m n=8 −67. ETH: 262 of 347 fires, gross +216.
- GATE under the new rules: fact-engine classes BLOCK at 1m/5m/15m on held-out (PF ≤ 1.25 with TP1-only provisional exits), 60m carried PASS; combos Fr+YB@1m (PF 1.52 < the 2.0 bar), Fr+YB@5m, FG+Fr+Vec@1m, Fr+Vec@1m all BLOCK — i.e. the spam combo is gated OUT. 1m fires fall from ~80/day live to ~2.5/day (186 gated 1m fires over 74 fire-days). Calibrated class exits: 1m TP1 22.25/SL 24.75 (cap — realized targets anchor at the box level, mostly 10–15 pts), 5m 11/16.75, 60m 23.25/17.75.
- fe-bt BUG FIXED IN PASSING: gridSearch's `net >= 0.85 × maxNet` eligibility band is EMPTY when every cell nets negative (0.85 × a negative best lies above it) → `reduce` of an empty array killed the whole run. Band is now `maxNet − 0.15·|maxNet|` and never empty. Any losing class under the new rules would have crashed the Sunday regen the same way.
- WINDOW_START_KEY in fe-bt is a hard-coded date (was 2026-04-15 = 5 months) — the "last 3 months" directive is only honoured if someone rolls it; the sandbox script rolls it per run. Make it rolling (today − 92 d) when the regen is next touched.

**2026-09-25 — B5 + C2 FOLLOW-UP (verifier refutations of the 2026-09-24 fix round): the browser tab was the one engine writer that was neither seeded nor order-gated; the C2 query dedupe had no test and one option leak**
- B5 WHAT WAS MISSING: the server writers (catch-up, live engine worker + inline) already fed `priorFires = loadPriorFires(now)` into runFactEngine, but the tab (client/src/pages/market.tsx) ran all three of its runFactEngine sites (viewed interval, hidden intervals, bg scanners) unseeded and its evaluateFormingBar call got no `openTrades` — ONE_OPEN_PER_DIRECTION never applied to intra-candle fires even though the engine supported it. And the tab discarded the persist POST's answer, so its auto-trader could POST /api/trade/execute for a fire the admission route had REFUSED.
- FIX: `shared/engine-seed.ts` is the ONE stored-row → PriorFire mapping (catchup.ts loadPriorFires now uses `priorsFromRows`); new `GET /api/signals/prior-fires/:symbol` returns exactly loadPriorFires, the tab polls it (30 s + after each of its own persists), keeps per-interval array identity (`stablePriors` — a same-content poll re-runs no engine memo) and feeds priorFires to every runFactEngine call + `lastFireTime`/`openTrades` (latest bracket per direction strictly before the forming bar, NaN-level rows cooldown-only) to evaluateFormingBar. ORDER ADMISSION twice: the tab records a verdict per posted key from the POST's `admissionKeys` and withholds refused/failed fires; the route itself (`orderSignalGateReason`, server/fire-admission.ts) refuses any fireTs order whose (interval, fireTs, direction) is not in signal_history — covers stale tabs running old JS. Test orders (no fireTs) unaffected.
- TESTABILITY: `buildLiveEngineContext({ fetchJson })` test seam — scripts/fire-admission.test.ts now builds the real context against a temp DB with a stub fetcher, so deleting the `loadPriorFires` line FAILS the suite (mutation-verified). A source-scan assert keeps COOLDOWN/ONE_OPEN/YB literals and the ICT/legacy constants out of the live-path writer modules (ICT_CONST.COOLDOWN_BARS spaces ICT setups only; shared/firing/constants COOLDOWN_BARS is the retired points model — both now say so).
- C2 (candle window dedupe): `windowQueryPlan` / `windowQueryKey` / `WINDOW_QUERY_OPTIONS` / `bg15mFrom5mData` in client/src/lib/candle-window.ts build every cached-continuous key on market.tsx; scripts/cached-continuous-cap.test.ts section 10 is the DOM-free fetch spy (distinct enabled keys == distinct URLs on every view × vector toggle; main key IS the shared 1m/5m/60m entry; bg15m == agg5mTo15m(5m entry), re-derived after a splice). LESSON: react-query applies EVERY observer's options to the shared Query — the bg 5m observer's default `retry: 3` silently overrode the main chart query's `retry:false` on the 5m view (and q1m/q60m on the 1m/60m views). One option object for all observers of a key.
- RULE: a seeded replay's output depends on DB state (first writer wins) — expected, documented in fire-admission.ts. Takes effect: client halves on tab reload (Vite); the endpoint + route gate need the owner's server restart (until then the tab's prior-fires fetch gets the SPA HTML → no seed, pre-B5 behaviour). Still open from the review: bug 4(a) mount-relative persist floor in market.tsx (the tab still re-posts its 2-day recompute on every mount; B2 provenance + admission now contain the damage) — browser-verify C2 only after it lands or keep relying on the spy test.

**2026-09-24 — FIRST REAL IBKR CONNECTION (live TWS 7496): what only a real broker taught us, and why the own-money route is parked**
- 10372 `lastTradeDateOrContractMonth: The date entered is invalid`: newer TWS builds return the field in contractDetails as `20261218 08:30:00 US/Central`; echoing it into reqTickByTickData / reqHistoricalData / placeOrder makes TWS refuse EVERY request (no ticks, no bars) — `requestDateField()` now sends only the yyyymmdd/yyyymm prefix (test: "LIVE 10372"). The mock could never have caught this: the fixture used the short form. RULE: the first live connection of any broker bridge is a test session, expect encoding surprises.
- 10189 `No market data permissions for CME FUT`: tick-by-tick needs the CME Real-Time (NP, L1) subscription; IBKR will NOT sell any market-data subscription to an account below ~USD 500 equity ("Insufficient equity" in Client Portal), and a deposit in transit does not count. History (reqHistoricalData) DID work without the subscription — 2 days of 1m bars, gap fills, the hello handshake and the order channel all worked on the live account; only live prices were blocked (and `execute()` correctly refused orders with no ticks).
- Reconnect path proven live: TWS exit/login → 502 ECONNREFUSED ×5 with 2→32 s backoff → reconnected, contract re-resolved, subscriptions re-requested, history re-delivered.
- IB system messages (1100/1101/1102/2110/farm 210x) arrive on `EventName.info`, not `error` — caught by the adjacent-fix review, wired via onInfo → onError(msg, code, -1).
- OUTCOME: the user will not fund USD 500 — and under that no broker can hold even one MES overnight (exchange margin ~USD 1.5k/contract; the strategy carries overnight by design). The own-money route is parked: `IB_ENABLED=false`, bridge dormant behind the flag, back to MotiveWave + a funded (prop) account. Prop-firm research (no activation fee, first-day payouts, automation + overnight + Rithmic/MotiveWave allowed) ran as a verified multi-lens sweep — see the 09-24 chat/journal for the ranking.
- OPS: a server started from the Claude preview pane dies with the session (09-22→09-23 outage); durable start = Desktop "Start Trading Server" → `C:\BaxterData\start-trading-server.bat` (supervisor, asks Y/N to restart a running server). `/api/live/bar` answers `{stale:true, reason:"no live ticks"}` whenever mw-reader has no tick for 60 s — before this a days-old MW seed price was served with a CURRENT timeSec and the phone pinned it onto the newest candle.
- DECISION (09-24 afternoon): after the verified prop-firm sweep (docs/prop-firm-research-2026-09-24.md — only DayTraders.com fits an automated overnight strategy; Lucid/Bulenox fit if flat-by-close), the user chose APEX again. Apex bans automation in writing and requires flat before 4:59 PM ET, so on Apex the AutoTrader stays DISARMED (the app is a signal/alert tool, trades placed by hand) and tracked carry-overnight outcomes overstate what Apex lets you execute. Offered: a server-side arm-lock for Apex mode + a 4:59 PM ET force-close outcome variant for honest accounting.
- OPS 2 (09-24 08:51/08:55): two `preview_start` boots died 4–12 s in with Windows exit 3221226356 (0xC0000374 STATUS_HEAP_CORRUPTION), silently, right after the synchronous boot chain (last line `[yahoo-live] fallback poller armed`); the SAME code booted fine a minute later from a plain shell (`npx tsx server/index.ts`) — worker up, :3000/:5000 listening — and a standalone probe opening the OneDrive-hosted DB on main + worker threads passed. So the crash is preview-launch-specific (stdio/console environment), not the code or the DB. RULE: when the pane boot dies with a native exit code, start from a shell / the Desktop shortcut before chasing the code; note the whole node_modules + data/app.db carry the OneDrive ReparsePoint attribute (native .node files included) — moving the project out of OneDrive is still the open reliability item.

**2026-09-23 — INTERACTIVE BROKERS BRIDGE (server/ibkr-bridge.ts, behind IB_ENABLED=true, default OFF): the three MotiveWave studies are only a JSON bridge, so the Node server now speaks that protocol itself against IB Gateway**
- WHAT SHIPPED: `server/live-bars.ts` refactored for reuse with ZERO behaviour change — the /ws/mw-feed message handler is now `ingestStudyMessage(msg, ctx)` and the /ws/order-commands event handler is `ingestAutoTraderEvent(msg)`; an ORDER-EXECUTOR REGISTRY (`registerOrderExecutor`) lets an in-process executor own `broadcastOrderCommand` / `isOrderCommandSocketOpen()` (only when registered AND ready — a disconnected Gateway reads as "AutoTrader not connected", honestly). `server/ibkr-bridge.ts` = IB Gateway ticks (tick-by-tick AllLast) + 1m TRADES keepUpToDate bars + connect-time bulk_bars/hello + gap-audit backfill servicing + MKT/LMT/STP GTC brackets (TP1-only, transmit chained) + AutoTrader-identical events + reconnect/heartbeat/1100-1102/10167 handling + `/api/ibkr/status` + digest line. `docs/ibkr-setup.md` is the owner's step-by-step (Client Portal → Gateway settings → paper → IBC → live). 155 asserts in `scripts/ibkr-bridge.test.ts` (in `npm test`), tsc clean. NOT verified against a real Gateway (the owner has no IB account yet) — see the RISKS below before the first paper session.
- WHAT WORKED (method): (1) extracting a 330-line handler PROGRAMMATICALLY (a node script that slices the exact body, re-indents, and asserts every socket-specific substring it replaces) and then proving equivalence with a whitespace-insensitive line MULTISET diff before/after — 15 removed lines, each with its relocated twin; hand-retyping would have drifted comments/edge cases. (2) Dependency injection for the bridge (api, ingest, emitEvent, executor registry, timers, persistence) so a mock IBApi — a plain EventEmitter with the same method/event names — drives every path without a Gateway; timers are captured and run by ms-filter. (3) Type-only imports (`import type … from "./live-bars"`) + lazy `import()` in `startIbkrBridge` keep the module free of db/live-bars at load, which is what makes it importable in a test that must not touch prod (`DB_PATH` → temp DB for the live-bars registry test).
- @stoqey/ib FACTS (1.6.10, read from dist/): `error` is emitted as `(Error, code, reqId, advancedOrderReject)`; `historicalDataUpdate` args are `(reqId, time, open, high, low, close, volume, count, WAP)` although the decoder READS them in a different order; `historicalData` ends with a sentinel whose `time` starts with `"finished"` and open=-1; `tickByTickAllLast` `time` is an epoch-seconds STRING; `TimeInForce`/`WhatToShow` are const objects not enums; the CJS package's named imports work under tsx/Node 24; the production esbuild bundle leaves it external (not in script/build.ts' allowlist — fine).
- DESIGN DECISIONS worth remembering: raw symbol keeps the MONTH CODE ("MESZ6", derived from IB's localSymbol or contractMonth) because contract-guard's roll watch keys on it; `order_filled.entry` is the COMMAND price (trade-state matches ±2 pts) with the real fill in `fillPrice`; child-fill reasons are AutoTrader's `tp_filled`/`sl_filled` (trade-notify formats those), not the task text's `tp1`/`sl`; a TP2 leg is NEVER placed even when a command says tp1Only=false (TP1-only policy wins — logged); backfill answers are limited to ranges AFTER the previous contract's expiry so an old-month hole can never be healed with new-month prices (the 09-17 interleave class); the declined `backfill_done` is deferred one tick because gap-audit calls `link.send` from inside its dispatcher (synchronous re-entry recursed per queued range).
- RISKS / UNVERIFIED (no Gateway to test against): (a) `reqHistoricalData("2 D", "1 min", keepUpToDate)` — IB's documented cap for 1-min bars is 1 D; the bridge retries with "1 D" on a 162/321 for that reqId. (b) `formatDate=2` with keepUpToDate updates — the parser also accepts the wall-clock forms. (c) MKT parent with `tif=GTC`+`outsideRth` — IB may warn (399, treated as info) or reject on some accounts; switch the parent to DAY if so. (d) IB's bracket OCA should cancel the sibling on a child fill; the bridge cancels a survivor itself after 2 s (a 10148 "already cancelled" reply is informational). (e) ROLL vs GUARD: the build's first default (8 d, the CME volume roll) would have rolled IB ~4 days before Yahoo's observed ES=F roll → the contract guard would quarantine the feed + block orders for those days EVERY quarter — the post-build audit changed the default to 4 d (Yahoo's roll; a few-hour mismatch fast-recovers). (g) DELAYED DATA ORDER GATE (post-build audit): `isReady()` did not consider IB 10167, so a paper account without the shared real-time subscription would have had the engine firing on ~15-min-old ticks into MKT brackets — `execute()` now refuses while `marketData === "delayed"` and every (re)connect resets the verdict so a fixed entitlement is picked up without a server restart. (f) `ibkr-brackets.json` (BAXTER_ARTIFACTS_DIR) is the restart memory for open brackets — delete it if the account was flattened by hand while the server was down.
- POST-BUILD REVIEW FIXES (2026-09-23, adversarial review — all covered in `scripts/ibkr-bridge.test.ts`, 245 asserts): (F1) BACKFILL ERA FLOOR — the floor was `prevLastTradeSec ?? 0`, but `reqContractDetails` uses includeExpired:false, so the day the old contract expired the floor collapsed to now − 30 d (IB would serve Dec prices into Sep-era holes → reconcile deletes + roll-heal's whole-history shift, the 09-17 class), and between the bridge's own roll and the old expiry the floor sat in the FUTURE (everything declined). Now `eraStartSec()` = max(seed, persisted): seed = 00:00 ET of the previous quarterly expiry (`prevLastTradeSec` while listed, else `thirdFridayUtcSec(month − 3)`) − min(rollDays, 4) d + 1 d margin (the store rolled with Yahoo, not with the bridge); persisted = the instant the bridge itself switched (`<BAXTER_ARTIFACTS_DIR>/ibkr-state.json` {eras}, via injectable loadState/saveState). Applied to backfill requests, chunk results AND the connect-time bulk_bars dump. (F2) POLICY DECLINES are `backfill_done {count:0, declined:true, reason:"era"|"max_days"|"disabled"}` — a plain count 0 made gap-audit mark the range `no_data` after two answers (seconds apart) and gap-heal never asked Yahoo (requestGapSweep was non-null). gap-audit now skips declined spans for 6 h (enqueue + dispatchNext), never marks them, and `takeStudyDeclined()` hands them to gap-heal's Yahoo path each pass (MotiveWave never sends `declined` — unchanged). (F3) `execute()` also refuses on IB connectivity lost (1100/2110) and when the last tick on THIS connection is > 90 s old or absent (isReady unchanged). (F4) a rejected CHILD leg: entry unfilled → cancel parent + sibling, bracket closed "rejected"; entry filled → a standalone replacement STP/LMT under a fresh id (remapped, loud order_error; "UNPROTECTED" if it throws); an entry fill that lands after the cancel re-opens the bracket with a replacement stop. (F5) every IB historical request has a 60 s no-answer timer (backfill chunk: keep partial bars / retry once / drop — backfill_done is ALWAYS delivered; live stream: cancel + re-request through the pacer); gap-audit's watchdog clears an in-flight range older than 180 s (deep probe 15 min) and re-queues it once at the front. (F6) request ids moved to 2,000,000,001+ (tick/bars/contract/exec/backfill bases) and onError resolves own request ids BEFORE tracked order ids — IB shares one id space. (F7) BarStream re-emits a late same-minute update within 120 s of the flush that closed it (`stats.barCorrections`; downstream is an idempotent upsert). (F8) execute()'s parse-failure / placeOrder-throw order_error is deferred one tick so the caller's trade record exists before its prune. (F10) the roll instant is 00:00 ET of the roll day (`etMidnightSec`), not UTC midnight − N d (= 19:00/20:00 ET the evening before, mid-ETH). DOCS: the owner installed TWS (live), not IB Gateway — ports TWS 7496 live / 7497 paper vs Gateway 4001 / 4002; "Enable ActiveX and Socket Clients" is a TWS-only checkbox (the Gateway API is always on); with Auto restart the daily restart needs no password (manual login after a cold start and every Sunday; IBC automates both but not the IB Key phone tap). NO PAPER ACCOUNT (owner's choice): soak on LIVE with Read-Only API checked — TWS/Gateway refuses every order at the source while ticks/bars/backfill/status all work — then uncheck + restart the server. The owner got stuck on "set IB_ENABLED=true + IB_PORT in .env, restart the server": the doc now says where `.env` is, how to open it in Notepad, which lines to change, and how to restart via the Desktop shortcut — never assume the owner knows what a dotenv file is.
- REAL-MONEY GAP FIXES (2026-09-23, G1–G6 — each covered by a test that FAILS with the fix reverted, verified by a mutation run; 313 asserts): (G1) placeOrder throwing on the TP or SL leg AFTER the parent MKT was sent used to untrack the bracket and leave a sent parent at IB with no stop — now every sent leg is `cancelOrder`ed, the bracket stays tracked (`placeFailed`) until IB reports the parent Cancelled/10147 (→ closed 'rejected') or Filled (→ the F4 replacement STP), and a deferred order_error says so ("cancelOrder FAILED … WITHOUT A STOP" if the cancel throws too). (G2) an informational 2100–2999 warning carrying a historical reqId (2174 date-time zone, 2176 …) is logged and the request + its timer are KEPT — it used to drop the live stream (→ 1 D retry) and decline every backfill chunk; `ibDateTime` sends IB's explicit-UTC dash form `yyyymmdd-HH:MM:SS`, which never provokes 2174. (G3) a 2110 loss also clears on a farm '…is OK' (2104/2106/2158) — IB often sends no 1101/1102 for it, so the F3 gate refused orders until a reconnect; a 1100 loss still waits for 1101/1102.
- (G4) the 60 s historical no-answer timer is re-armed on EVERY historicalData row (live + backfill), so it measures silence — a slow 2-day opening batch streaming past 60 s was cancelled and restarted from scratch. (G5) a contract roll cancels the in-flight backfill request, clears the queue and answers every pending job `backfill_done {declined:true, reason:'roll', count:0}` next tick — old-month bars already held are never emitted under the new symbol. (G6) RULE: an incomplete backfill job (a chunk that timed out with partial rows, went silent twice, or failed with anything but 162 no-data) is DECLINED ('incomplete'), NEVER partially answered — a partial count>0 answer lets gap-audit's reconcile delete the real stored rows of the missing span.
- G3/F3 WERE DEAD CODE AGAINST A REAL TWS (verifier refutation, 2026-09-23): @stoqey/ib 1.6.10's decoder (`decodeMsg_ERR_MSG`) routes EVERY ERR_MSG with id -1 to `EventName.info(message, code)`, never `EventName.error` — and IB sends 1100/1101/1102/2110 and the 210x/2157/2158 farm statuses with id -1. The bridge listened only on `error`, so the 1100 order refusal + push, the 1101 resubscribe, the 2110 loss and the farm '…is OK' recovery never ran; only the 90 s tick-age gate guarded orders. The suite passed because MockIB emitted those codes on 'error'. FIX: `wire()` subscribes `EventName.info` → `onInfo()` → `onError(msg, code, -1)` (501 'already connected' is library-local → log only). TESTS: MockIB.sys() emits system codes the library's way and records any id -1 system code emitted on 'error' as a fidelity violation (asserted zero); a new block feeds raw ERR_MSG frames through a REAL (never-connected, no socket) IBApi decoder to pin the routing and drives the bridge end-to-end through it (2110 → 2104 OK → tick → order; 1100 → push + refusal; 1101 resubscribe; 1102). Mutation (info listener removed) → 20 asserts fail. LESSON: a mock must reproduce the LIBRARY's event shapes, not the wire protocol's — verify routing against the real decoder, not the docs. The 1100-is-not-cleared-by-farm-OK narrowing is deliberate (errs toward refusing orders). 331 asserts.

**2026-09-18 — "HAVING TROUBLE STAYING LIVE / LAGGY" + A FROZEN SERVER: four independent causes, all measured, all fixed (engine pass → worker thread; safe-stdio; shared response cache; heap caps) + the contract guard re-architected to BROADCAST-TIME translation**
- MEASURE FIRST (the probe that found everything): hit a pure in-memory route (`GET /api/live/bar`) every 250 ms for a few minutes and group the slow samples. BEFORE: p50 365 ms · p90 7.6 s · p99 12 s · 33 timeouts — the single server thread was blocked about half the time, so ticks/WS broadcasts queued for seconds ("laggy"). AFTER: 936/936 answered · p50 2 ms · p90 3 ms · p99 244 ms · max 560 ms. "Is the chart laggy" is an event-loop question before it is a chart question.
- CAUSE 1 — the server-side live engine (2026-08-13) ran its pass ON THE MAIN THREAD every minute: 6–10 s each (profiled: context build 1.9 s, `runEngineForInterval` 1m 8.2 s / 5m 3.3 s / 15m 1.2 s / 60m 0.3 s — the ENGINE RUN is the block, not JSON.parse), plus an 18 s catch-up every 10 min. FIX: `server/live-engine-worker.ts` — `computeLivePass()` is the ONE implementation used by both the worker and the inline fallback; orders/persist/gates/claims stay on the main thread (main-thread cost now 0–32 ms per pass). WHAT FAILED: `new Worker(new URL("./x.ts", import.meta.url))` — Workers DO inherit tsx's execArgv but tsx's loader skips registration off the main thread (isMainThread guard) → "Cannot find package '@shared/…'". WHAT WORKED: an eval'd CJS bootstrap that calls `require(<tsx/esm/api>).register()` then `import(entry)`. The worker must import ONLY `./catchup` + `@shared/*` (gap-audit has module-load setIntervals; live-engine pulls it in via live-bars). Also fixed honestly: `lastRunBoundary` was stamped BEFORE the pass, so a bar that landed late was never evaluated live (the header comment claiming otherwise was false) — a boundary is now done only when the newest 1m ≥ boundary − 60 (or after 5 min); nothing-changed ticks skip on a 7 µs `MAX(timestamp)` read.
- CAUSE 2 — MEMORY: the machine has 16 GB and had 0.4 GB free; the server sat at 3.0 GB because V8's default multi-GB limit never made it collect (measured: a full four-interval pass needs ~450 MB RSS and drops to ~15 MB used after GC). Paging made EVERYTHING slow (a 0.4 s day-zones call took 18.8 s). FIX: worker `resourceLimits.maxOldGenerationSizeMb: 768`, `npm run dev` → `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=2048`, node-cache checkperiod 30 → 5 s, deep-history bodies (>12 MB) not cached. Server now ~600–750 MB.
- CAUSE 3 — `cached-continuous` never hit its cache: every consumer sends `from = now − N days` to the SECOND, so the key changed per call; each tab's 15 s reconcile + each hidden engine page's four 90-day windows + the engine's own four windows were each a fresh read + filter + 8.8 MB `JSON.stringify`. FIX: floor `from` to the hour (query AND key), cache the SERIALIZED body, raw prepared read instead of the drizzle row mapper; every bar writer now `cacheInvalidate`s after its commit (mw-reader's tick-built persists did not).
- CAUSE 4 — periodic hogs: `auditGaps` read all ~2.46M MES 1m rows into a Set and walked every minute since 2019 (~4.5 s, 2–3× per 10-min gap-heal pass); the Yahoo-lag tail + the forming bucket were "gaps" every pass, which ran the 26k-row `yahooBackfillSymbol` for 1m holes it can never fill. FIX: `{sinceTs}` bound (gap-heal 7 d) + merge-walk (247 synthetic cases identical to the old walk; 122 ms vs 749 ms), forming bucket never audited, 15-min tail grace, coarse-only heavy backfill, permanent Yahoo holes (18:00 ET open minute, 00:00–00:09 ET) retired as `yahoo_no_data` after 2 covered-but-empty passes (MW-path audits ignore the marker), heal from ES=F not MES=F (Yahoo rolls each continuous ticker on its own schedule — a Yahoo-vs-Yahoo interleave the guard cannot see), per-bar broadcasts for ≤10 healed bars, first pass at +3 min.
- THE 12:01 OUTAGE (server frozen at 0 % CPU, answering nothing, 23 min): on Windows a console/pipe write is SYNCHRONOUS; the server had been launched by a session whose reader went away, the stdout pipe filled (~80 KB — under a minute, >90 % of it the 20/s `/api/live/bar` log line) and the next `console.log` blocked forever. Reproduced in `scripts/safe-stdio.test.ts` (control child's heartbeat dies within a second; `process.stdout._handle.setBlocking(false)` returns 0 and does NOT help on Node 24). FIX: `server/safe-stdio.ts` (FIRST import of index.ts) — console writes go to a writer THREAD (bounded backlog, drops beyond it) + an async local file log `C:\BaxterData\logs\server-YYYYMMDD.log` (the first durable server log this project has had — there was NOTHING to read from the wedged process). Caveat: while the writer thread is stuck `process.exit()` cannot finish — kill from outside. Same incident: `spawnCapture`'s timeout killed only the cmd.exe wrapper (`shell:true`) — the integrity-check grandchild lived 19 min and the awaiting digest never resolved → `killProcessTree` (taskkill /T /F) + resolve from the timeout path; the digest no longer reports yesterday's integrity JSON after a timed-out run. ALSO OBSERVED: the killed process lingered as a zombie and the first restart died with `SQLITE_IOERR_TRUNCATE` — `data/app.db*` carry the OneDrive ReparsePoint attribute (the live 1.6 GB DB + 150 MB WAL sit inside a OneDrive-synced folder). Not fixed here (moving the DB is the user's call — DB_PATH is supported); it is the next reliability item.
- CONTRACT GUARD RE-ARCHITECTED (adversarial design review of yesterday's ingest-point translation): translate at BROADCAST time, not ingest. mw-reader's in-memory bars + `lastTickPrice` stay RAW — MW's basis, the basis brackets are worked in, and the only MW price the verdict pairs (so translation can never feed back into its trigger, a verdict flip needs no bar reset, and the REST bypasses close for free); the frozen spread is added only in `wireBar()` / the tick broadcast / `GET /api/live/bar`; translated bars are `provisional` (market.tsx must never evaluate one as a bar close — it would evaluate bucket T twice, once translated and once ~10 min later on Yahoo's canonical bar). YAHOO'S CME CHART FEED IS ~10 MINUTES DELAYED — that, not the poll cadence, is why a Yahoo-driven chart can never feel live; its `{type:"tick"}` (a ten-minute-old print the clients painted into the live candle) is gone, and its still-forming coarse buckets are withheld while MW's translated ticks drive. New: month code taken from the TICK stream only + 20-tick debounce (per-message handling flapped at message rate with relays on two months), MIXED-MONTHS quarantine, tick-vote roll detection, FAST RECOVERY when month-code change + votes agree, trip PURGE of the MW-written rows of the detection window (Yahoo's DO-NOTHING writer would never overwrite them — a guaranteed ~15-min interleave at every roll otherwise), frozen offset re-centred only on ≥1-pt drift × 4, wrong-basis FREEZE in trade-state (`basisSuspect`: a bracket that lived through a mismatch is never price-inferred, now or after recovery — the 09-17 phantom `tp1_hit` +$643.75 in the Apex tracker was exactly that), gap-audit gated + tainted backfills never reconcile-delete.
- CLIENT (terminal + hidden engine page + phone): server-computed `ageMs` stale-tick guard; a non-live bar can no longer overwrite a live close in the same-bucket merge; `contract_guard` events reset the straddling forming candle; ranged `data_updated {fromTs}` (a bare one re-downloaded the whole scrolled-back history on every heal/flip); `candlesRevision` so an interior bar REPLACED by reconcile repaints (the lw-charts fast path's "same length/first/last" is also the signature of an in-place replacement); no-op reconciles return the same array; MarketView HUD stats memoized (it did O(dataset) work per tick).
- METHOD NOTES: (1) three agents edited ONE working tree in parallel safely because file ownership was disjoint and the WS message contract was written down first; (2) a harness for server modules without touching prod: `DB_PATH` → empty temp DB + a PORT proxy that forwards GETs and swallows POSTs; (3) `preview_logs` search does not take regex alternation — grep the durable log file instead; (4) the hidden browser pane throttles long timers — install a `window.__probe` WS listener in one call and read it in the next; (5) bash heredocs carrying apostrophes/backticks failed in this harness — write patch scripts with the Write tool and run them with node.
- STATE AT HAND-OFF: MW rolled to MESZ6 (December) — guard on-contract, feed mw-live; auto-trade is DISARMED (boot-disarm after today's restarts) — re-arm from Settings when ready. Pre-existing and NOT touched: the 2 quality-gate config test failures, the 12 integrity violations from the Aug 7–14 rows vs the 08-12 book (the Sunday regen has been failing since 08-16).

**2026-09-17 — "THE CHARTING BUG IS BACK": it was the QUARTERLY CONTRACT ROLL, not a chart defect — Yahoo ES=F rolled to DEC at 09-14 11:30 ET while the MW chart stayed on SEP (expiring 09-18); two feeds wrote two contract months into the same MES key for three sessions (server/contract-guard.ts + server/roll-repair.ts shipped)**
- SYMPTOM vs CAUSE: the user's screenshot showed the 5m chart stopped at ~06:20 ET with a 7688 last price while the clock read 10:05 — a stale tab (PC sleep + the 09:56 restart; a fresh load rendered current bars). The REAL defect underneath: since 09-14 11:31 ET the 1m store alternated Sep prints (~7620, MW tick-built, V=tick counts) and Dec prints (~7687, Yahoo, real volume) — 46 ±67-pt open-vs-prev-close flips in 1m, folded by derive into 5m/15m/60m; the engine read the sawtooth as violent reactions (126 "live" 1m fires on 09-16, 69 today, 108/126 "wins" from 66-pt phantom TP hits) with AutoTrader ARMED (5 contracts). Decisive evidence: `ESU26.CME` 7619 vs `ESZ26.CME`/`ES=F` 7685.5 at the same minute (spread 66.5 = Sep/Dec carry); the store's 09:57 1m bar opened −68 from 09:56 at the exact second the restart handed the feed from the Yahoo heal (934 bars) back to MW ticks.
- WHY NO GUARD CAUGHT IT: `normalizeSymbol` strips month codes BY DESIGN (MESU6/MESZ6/ES=F → MES) and every liveness check (`isMwActive`, audits, gap-heal) asked "is MW sending?" — never "does MW AGREE with the other writer?". roll-heal.ts only handles MW's own back-adjusted continuous re-basing (constant δ across an overlap of one source), not two sources on two months.
- FIX 1 — CONTRACT GUARD (server/contract-guard.ts): pairs MW's completed 1m closes (finalizeBar1m + LiveBarRelay 1m bars) with Yahoo ES=F same-minute closes from its own ~60s reference poll (independent of yahoo-live, which is idle while MW is authoritative). Verdict = pure `assessPairs` over the NEWEST 4 pairs: trip when all |Δ| ≥ 10 pts, same sign, spread ≤ 8 (constant roll-spread signature — a 10-pt ES-vs-MES divergence for 3 straight minutes is impossible); recover when all |Δ| ≤ 4. While off-contract: MW tick/bar/forming broadcasts + persistence gated (mw-reader finalize*, live-bars tick/bar/bulk_bars), yahoo-live's `isMwActiveReal` treats MW as absent (Yahoo writes + broadcasts), gap-heal never dispatches to the study, and ORDERS ARE BLOCKED at `broadcastOrderCommand` + both order paths (`orderContractGateReason`). Flips → push+Discord (`contract_guard` notify type), `contract_guard` WS broadcast, persisted in app_settings so a restart never re-admits wrong-month bars (seeded OFF before today's restart). Surfaces: sync-status `contractGuard`, red header pill "MW OFF-CONTRACT · Δ −66.50 · YAHOO DRIVING" (FeedStatusBadge), digest "Contract guard:" line, integrity-check **V6-contract-interleave** (≥30-pt 1m flip-flops in 7d). 18/18 unit asserts (scripts/contract-guard.test.ts, in `npm test`).
- FIX 2 — REPAIR (server/roll-repair.ts, `POST /api/data/roll-mismatch-repair {fromTs?, apply}`; apply defaults to a DRY RUN): Yahoo ES=F is canonical; every in-window 1m minute Yahoo can legally write (validateBar + isSessionOpen) is OVERWRITTEN; stored minutes Yahoo CANNOT write are judged against the nearest Yahoo close within 10 min and deleted only when ≥ 10 pts off (closed-session prints deleted regardless — no ingest guard admits them); derive re-runs; native coarse rows in 1m-less buckets get the same judgment; `yellowbox_day_zones` rows from the window's day-key are dropped (07-29 degenerate-cache rule); caches flushed + data_updated. Everything touched goes to `cached_candles_roll_backup_20260917` first (row-identity dedup). RESULT: window 09-14 11:59 ET → now, 4,020 minutes rewritten (669 were Sep-priced), 16 orphan 1m + 3+3+2 coarse rows deleted, 5,259 rows backed up; ≥30-pt flips 46 → 0 in every resolution (only Yahoo's own one-way roll cliff at 09-14 11:31 remains, which is correct for a non-back-adjusted continuous series). Verified minute-by-minute vs ESZ26: store == Dec across 09-16 11:00 → 09-17 10:30.
- TWO CLASSIFIER GOTCHAS (each cost one re-run): (a) Yahoo's newest minute and some pre-halt minutes arrive with VOLUME 0 (its volume lags the print) — the ghost guard rejects the bar, so "Yahoo has this minute" must be defined as "Yahoo can WRITE this minute", or the wrong-month row silently survives (10:24 today); Yahoo's close is still a fine price REFERENCE for the judgment. (b) auto-detect finds the FIRST disagreeing minute — after a partial repair that is the newest stragglers, so pass `fromTs` explicitly on re-runs. Also: Yahoo's 1m chart feed was ~10 min behind real time today (regularMarketTime lag), so the fallback chart lags that much while MW is quarantined and the guard's pairing ring had to be widened to 30 min (restart to load).
- SIGNAL ROWS: the fe-bt window regen KEEPS `source='live'` rows on collision ("stored LIVE record stands") — the corrupted live fires (343) survived the first regen. Runbook order that works: back up (`signal_history_roll_backup_20260917`, 604 rows) → purge ALL window rows → `--window-from 2026-09-14T11:59 --persist --skip-xlsx` (clean: 15m 2 / 1m 309 / 5m 72 / 60m 9, 0 collisions) → `POST /api/scheduler/catchup-now` (150 fires == 150 rows). Integrity-check V6 clean; the 12 violations it still reports are PRE-EXISTING (Aug 7–14 rows vs the 08-12 book — the Sunday regen has been failing since 08-16 per `scheduler_last_regen`), not this window.
- LIVE-MONEY CONSEQUENCE (reported to the user, NOT acted on): two real MESU6 brackets were placed this morning off the corrupted series (1m Long 7628.75 tp 7642 sl 7603.75 ×5, 5m Long 7627 ×5); the server's trade-state later marked the 1m one `tp1_hit` against DEC bars (7700) — a phantom; the real Sep position is 66 pts lower and expires 09-18 09:30 ET. Server-side trade state is untrustworthy for a wrong-month position — manage it in MW. Auto-trade is DISARMED after the restart (boot-disarm safety) and would be blocked by the guard anyway until the MW chart is rolled to MESZ6.
- NEXT ROLL (Dec → Mar, ~2026-12-10..14): when Yahoo's ES=F switches, the guard trips, the pill goes red, orders block, Yahoo drives (~1–10 min behind) — roll the MW chart that day and everything resumes automatically. If MW rolls FIRST, the same happens in reverse for the few days until Yahoo rolls (consistent data, no MW ticks) — acceptable by design; the repair endpoint exists if anything ever interleaves again.

**2026-08-20 (late day) — GUARD DAY RE-ANCHORED TO THE RTH OPEN (user: "best solution for the drawdown fail safe... i missed a lot of trades because we were confused since i used the sim account; not trading eth for a while — keep it clear for rth sessions")**
- **Root of the confusion, precisely:** the guard day rolled at 18:00 ET (Globex), so an EVENING sim session booked into the NEXT MORNING's tracker — Wednesday-night sim losses paused Thursday's real RTH orders. The fix is the day boundary, not more resets: **the guard day now rolls at 9:25 AM ET** — anything booked in an evening (deliberate sim runs included) EXPIRES at the next morning's roll; every RTH day opens with a clean $2,500/$500 tracker automatically. Manual POST /api/trade/guard/reset stays for mid-day corrections.
- **Order window trimmed to [09:30–15:15] per the no-ETH directive** (the 4:00–7:30 European window removed — its record stays scored weekly; it can earn re-entry when the user resumes ETH). With no overnight orders at all, the tracker structurally cannot be polluted while the user sleeps.
- **Honest limit restated:** the guard is a per-DAY allowance; Apex's real trailing threshold is lifetime-anchored and lives in RTrader. After losing days the account's true remaining distance can be UNDER $2,500 — apexHeadroomDollars must be lowered to match on those mornings, or the guard is more permissive than the account.
- Verified: tsc 0; restart; orderWindows persisted [09:30–15:15]; today's guard day carried mid-day (correct — the new anchor takes effect at tomorrow's 9:25 roll); active 2-lot Short rehydrated; boot-disarmed (re-arm reminded).

**2026-08-20 (midday) — ETH STUDY → ORDER-HOURS WINDOW SHIPPED (user-approved) + the sim-night guard-pollution diagnosis**
- **The study (TP1-only era book, closed signals):** RTH n=355 82.5% win +1,250.7 PF 1.83 vs ETH n=519 59.9% win **−1,975.6** PF 0.61 — every ETH interval negative, every RTH interval positive. By ET hour: 18:00–03:00 is the kill zone (19:00 9% win PF 0.05, 20:00 **0% win**, 00:00 PF 0.20, 02:00 PF 0.28); **04:00–07:30 (European morning) is genuinely good** (PF ~4, 90%+ win, n≈120); 08:00 hour is a trap (PF 0.36 — 8:30 data releases); inside RTH the edge lives 9:30–14:00 (14:00 flat PF 0.96, 15:00–15:15 PF 0.34). Per-night: 4 of 6 ETH sessions negative (−260/−1,070/−1,214/−1,505); +937 (08-18) real; +1,136 (08-13) distrusted (rebase-day artifact). The user's manual habit (real account off overnight) was saving ~$10k/wk/contract vs the book.
- **Ship: `orderHoursEnabled` (ON) + `orderWindows` [04:00–07:30, 09:30–15:15 ET]** — gate in BOTH order paths (live-engine placeOrder incl. late entries, /api/trade/execute); signals/records fire 24/5 unchanged; empty window list = fail-OPEN by design (an emptied list must not silently kill all trading); HH:MM-validated via POST /api/trade/settings. Blocked zones stay scored weekly and can earn their way in.
- **"Why aren't signals going through" (same conversation): the Apex guard was pausing on SIM losses.** The user runs overnight auto-trading against a SIM account (real account off at night) — the guard tracker can't see which account MW points at, booked the sim night's −$2,117, crossed the $2,000 budget, and correctly paused the REAL day (engine skip reason verbatim in status). Fix shipped: **POST /api/trade/guard/reset** zeroes today's tracker (used after verifying real headroom in RTrader); the order-hours window prevents the recurrence structurally (no overnight orders → no overnight pollution). NOTE told to the user: the window gates ALL auto-trader orders, sim included — flip orderHoursEnabled off for a deliberate sim night.
- Verified: tsc 0; restart; settings + windows served; guard reset to 0/0; boot-disarmed (re-arm reminded).

**2026-08-20 — LATE ENTRY AT LEVEL SHIPPED (user directive: "if theres a catch up signal and it comes back to the same price level and the signal still shows that it will hit, make the trade")**
- **What it does:** every live-engine pass, recent DB rows (≤6h, this window is also the order-claim TTL bound) whose outcome is still open AND outside the fresh-fire window become resting entry intents: viability re-proven from served 1m bars (ANY TP-or-SL touch since the signal's entry bar kills the intent — a touched TP is a missed win, never chased; a touched SL is a loss, never chased), and when the live price is back within `lateEntryTolerancePts` (1.0) of the ORIGINAL entry, placeOrder fires with the ORIGINAL bracket. Every gate applies unchanged (intervals/direction/sameDirectionOnly/net-cap/apex-guard/claim); the 15:15–17:00 ET wall applies to late fills too. Settings: lateEntryEnabled/-TolerancePts/-MaxAgeMin (server-authoritative, POST /api/trade/settings).
- **RESTART SAFETY (caught in review before deploy):** the claim registry is in-memory — after a reboot an already-ordered still-open signal would have RE-FIRED through the late-entry path. The persisted actives list is the cross-restart truth: a tracked open trade matching entry(±0.26)/direction/interval skips the candidate. Any future order path must carry the same guard.
- **Honest framing (told to the user):** this closes the record-vs-account gap (the book assumes entry AT level; we now fill at that level when it retouches) but cannot make them identical — a time-shifted fill rides a different path (MFE already spent, different session context). The book's outcomes remain the book's.
- **Deploy note:** at the 11:47 AM restart the Apex-guard tracker (day 2026-08-20, clean since the 18:00 roll — yesterday's +1706 phantom pollution self-cleared at the roll as designed) read realized −$2,117.50 / peak +15 → used ≈ $2,132 ≥ the $2,000 budget → the guard was ALREADY PAUSING orders pre-restart, $383 before the Apex floor. Reported to the user with the RTrader cross-check instruction rather than silently overridden — the guard doing its job is not a bug.

**2026-08-18 (evening) — DOMAIN CHART WEDGE FIXED ("every time i try trading.jacksonlems.com for the chart it doesnt work"): the THIRD one-shot-probe wedge, this time in useTerminalData's candle fetch**
- **Diagnosis chain (all text-tool, browser pane):** domain page loads, API reads 200, live-bars WS opens through the tunnel (1.7s), yet ALL 17 chart canvases measured 0×0 with the lightweight-charts hosts 0-sized — while the SAME code on localhost rendered fine. A fresh domain reload rendered fine too → not the path, the FIRST LOAD: the tunnel intermittently 502s an early request (one caught live), and `useTerminalData.fetchCandles` was a ONE-SHOT — its catch did `setCandles([])` and nothing ever refetched (candles have no safety poll; signals do). Phone + tunnel = flaky first fetch more often than not → "every time". Also latent: no `res.ok` check — any non-JSON error body read as a throw, same wedge.
- **Fix (the JournalView 2026-08-07 / LedgerView 2026-08-17 pattern, now in its third home):** fetchCandles failures retry on a 4s→8s→16s→30s-cap backoff, generation-guarded (symbol/interval/reload switches cancel the pending retry + reset the backoff), plus an explicit `!res.ok` throw so tunnel 502s never read as "no data". Signals fetch left alone deliberately — it already keeps the displayed set on transient failure and the 120s safety poll heals it.
- **RULE (three instances make it law):** ANY mount-time fetch that gates a pane/chart MUST have a retry path — "the user can press reload" is a wedge, not a fallback. When a symptom is "works local, breaks on the domain", suspect the FIRST-LOAD failure mode through the tunnel before suspecting the serving path: measure canvases/hosts via javascript_tool, then reload and compare.

**2026-08-18 (afternoon) — APEX GUARDS SHIPPED (user: "is there any way you can help prevent it"): net-contract cap + trailing-threshold order pause + rejection auto-disarm — all ORDER-side, signals/records untouched**
- **Mechanics learned (50K Apex, verified against Apex 2026 tables + the actual price path):** contract cap 10 full/100 micros (25 MES was LEGAL size); the killer is the $2,500 trailing threshold, live from the FIRST trade on a brand-new account, whose floor ratchets up with every INTRADAY equity peak INCLUDING open profit and never comes down. Today's path: shorts avg 7719.65 peaked ~+$800 open at the 7713.25 low (floor +$800), then the 7728.75 reversal = peak-to-trough ~$2,250 + a day of fees (366 execution records) ≈ the full $2,500 → 12:22:36 liquidation at −$1,042.50 realized. At 25 micros ($125/pt), 12 pts of headroom-from-peak is the WHOLE account budget.

VERIFIED THROUGH THE EDGE: no key → 401; with key → 200 full sync-status; WS without key → refused. RULE: anything that fronts this server publicly ships WITH the gate in the same change — never "auth later".

**2026-08-04 — SIDE PROJECT: frat-game/ (KEG PLEDGE — Three.js FPS mini-game, port 5200, launch.json name "frat-game")**
- Unrelated to trading; mirrors omniverse-game's Vite+Three setup. Three transferable lessons: (1) **rAF pauses when the Browser pane isn't displayed** — headless verification of a game loop needs the loop refactored into a manually steppable `step(dt)` exposed on a debug hook (`window.FRAT.step`), driven via javascript_tool; screenshots stay impossible until the user displays the pane. (2) **Threshold-check-vs-decay ordering bug class:** a meter that's clamped at its trigger value (drunk=100) and decays every frame can NEVER satisfy `>= 100` if increments land between frames and the decay runs before the check — run lose-condition checks BEFORE per-frame drift, and let increments overshoot the clamp. (3) **three.js FPS movement:** camera yaw 0 looks down **-Z**; rotating local input (fx,fz) by yaw is `vx = fx·cos+fz·sin, vz = fz·cos−fx·sin` — the sign-flipped variant "works" at one spawn orientation and breaks everywhere else, so test strafe at yaw=π/2, not just spawn.
- npm `allow-scripts` policy blocks esbuild's postinstall in new subprojects — harmless: the platform binary arrives via `@esbuild/win32-x64` optional dep and vite runs fine.
- (2026-08-05 follow-up, act 2 + themed interiors) **Test-state contamination via reused DOM handlers:** a single shared card button whose `onclick` persists after dismissal means clicking it "blind" in a test script replays the LAST assigned handler (here: a respawn instead of the expected reset) — verification scripts must check the card is actually visible before clicking, and a "failed" result after such a click is suspect; re-running the same flow from clean state exonerated the game logic.

**2026-08-04 — CONCURRENT-TRADE INCIDENT (7 naked lots on the REAL Apex account) → AUTOTRADER v20: PER-BRACKET OCO ISOLATION (BracketState list, targeted sibling cancels — a fill NEVER touches another trade's orders)**

**2026-08-03 — AUTOTRADER v19: fill-event OCO bracket manager (onOrderFilled) — a TP/SL fill flattens the sibling leg INSTANTLY + split-mode stop resize + native-trail double-stop fix + the JDK-25-vs-Java-26-SDK compile recipe**
- **The defect (user report + log proof):** AutoTrader submitted the bracket legs UNLINKED (MW SDK has no native OCO) and relied solely on `onPositionClosed` → cancel-all — which can lag the fill by MINUTES (2026-08-03: tp1Only entry placed 13:19:50, onPositionClosed 13:34:50). In that window the full-qty stop keeps WORKING while flat — a touch doesn't flatten anything, it OPENS a fresh position. Split mode was worse: after TP1 half-fills, the still-full-qty stop would FLIP the position on an SL hit. And `trySubmitTrailingStop` submitted the native trail WITHOUT cancelling the fixed SL — two full-qty stops working simultaneously.
- **The fix (v19):** `onOrderFilled(OrderContext, Order)` is now the OCO manager: fill of any tracked exit leg → remaining qty computed from the KNOWN leg quantities (deterministic bookkeeping — `ctx.getPosition()` may not yet reflect the triggering fill, logged as cross-check only) → rest==0 ⇒ `cancelOrders()` ALL survivors immediately + `bracket_flattened` WS event; rest>0 (split-mode TP1) ⇒ stop resized via `Order.setAdjQuantity(int)` + resubmit (MW modify pattern), fallback cancel-single + recreate stop at the same price for the rest. Fills matching NO tracked leg are IGNORED (manual co-trading never triggers bracket logic). Trailer: the market exit is tracked via `trailerExitOrderRef` so its fill cancels the fixed SL; a successful native trailing stop now CANCELS the fixed SL and takes over as `slOrderRef`. `onPositionClosed` cancel-all retained as backstop. New WS events: `bracket_flattened{reason}`, `stop_reduced{qty}`; a REJECTED bracket leg alerts via `order_error`.
- **Compile recipe (MW updated the SDK to Java 26):** `mwave_sdk.jar` (dated Jun-29) is class-file v70 — javap AND javac from JDK 25 refuse it ("Unsupported class file version: 70"), so `build.bat` FAILS on this machine until JDK 26 is installed. Working recipe: copy the jar to scratch, patch each class's major-version bytes (offset 6–7) 70→69 (182 classes, small Node walker), then javap + `javac --release 17` against the PATCHED copy — emitted bytecode links by name, runtime-identical vs the real SDK. Signatures confirmed from the patched jar, not memory: `Study.onOrderFilled/onOrderCancelled/onOrderRejected/onOrderModified(OrderContext, Order)`; `OrderContext.getPosition()/cancelOrders(Order...)/cancelOrders()`; `Order.setAdjQuantity(int)`.
- **Source-vs-jar drift caught:** `menu="Custom"` (the 2026-06-26 discoverability fix) was MISSING from AutoTrader.java — it lived only in a since-overwritten build (the installed Jul-5 jar lacks it too; the study stayed usable only because it was already ON the chart). Re-added to the header. RULE: after any fix that "edited the source and rebuilt the jar", diff the SOURCE in git — a fix that exists only in a binary is one rebuild away from silently vanishing.
- VERIFIED: compile EXIT=0 (`--release 17`, class major 61); v19 banner + Custom menu present in the built class; jar 16,803 bytes at mw-study/AutoTrader.jar. DEPLOYED LIVE same day (user away, remote-driven): flat + Orders(0) confirmed on-screen BEFORE shutdown; MW close needed THREE dialogs (shutdown confirm → "Active Strategies!" confirm → v7.0.28 update prompt — declined, take deliberately); jar installed; relaunch stops at the WORKSPACE PICKER (click Continue); v19 initialize + WS connected on all instances; feed mw-live 4/4.
- **MW restart ops gotchas (all hit tonight):** (1) strategies come back DEACTIVATED after restart — click Activate on the strategy panel (account preselected), `onActivate(OrderContext)` in the log is the proof it's armed; (2) background chart tabs load LAZILY — their LiveBarRelay studies don't reconnect until each tab is clicked once (sync-status stuck at 1/4 until then); (3) the post-reconnect backfill triggers a WAVE of chart reloads that RE-INITIALIZE studies (9 re-inits over ~1 min, ~15s apart) and TRANSIENTLY show the strategy deactivated — do NOT fight it mid-wave; MW re-fires onActivate itself once the wave quiesces (~120s quiet), verify AFTER; (4) an invisible Windows "Click to Do" overlay (clicktodo.exe) intermittently sits frontmost and eats clicks — `taskkill //IM clicktodo.exe //F` is safe, it respawns on demand.
- **FIELD-VERIFIED LIVE 2026-08-04 10:01 ET (first v19 trade):** Long 5 @ 7676.25 (15m signal, tp1Only) → entry fill logged `entry filled — OCO manager armed (slQty=5 tp1Qty=5 tp2Qty=0)` → TP1 filled 5 @ 7687.50 → `OCO after fill: leg=TP1 rest=0 (ctx position=0)` → `OCO FLATTEN (tp_filled)` → SL stop `onOrderCancelled` **69 ms** after the TP fill (v18's onPositionClosed path took ~15 MINUTES for the same cleanup the prior day). Note: ctx position read 0 at the fill callback on this trade — but on TRADE 3 (12:32 ET, TP1 5 @ 7747) it read **1** mid-update (`OCO after fill: leg=TP1 rest=0 (ctx position=1)`): trusting getPosition() there would have "resized" the stop and left it WORKING while flat. The leg-qty bookkeeping as the sole decision input is VALIDATED live — getPosition() is cross-check logging only, never the decider.
- **Trade 2 (same day, 11:02 ET) proved the callback RACE is real:** MW delivered `onPositionClosed` 43 ms BEFORE the TP1 `onOrderFilled` (opposite order vs trade 1, and vs v18's 15-minute lag the prior day — the ordering/latency is nondeterministic). The backstop path did the cancel (SL `onOrderCancelled` +35 ms); `handleFill` then saw `activeBracket==null` and no-oped. RULE: BOTH cancel paths (fill-event + position-closed) are required, not belt-and-braces — either can land first.
- Confidence: high — deployed, armed, and field-verified on two live winning trades (one per cleanup path).

**2026-07-30 — APP-WIDE LAG ROOT-CAUSED: the 3.2s cached-days query (NOT the signals GET) + hidden-engine recompute storms; fixed via day-cache, fast ET clock, incremental signal_new, bg-memo hygiene**
- **MEASUREMENT LESSONS (the reported bottleneck was mis-attributed — always isolate before optimizing):** "GET /api/signals/history/MES/5m = 2.2s" was event-loop QUEUING, not handler time — the isolated handler measured 3ms warm (63 rows). Two artifacts poison endpoint timings on this box: (1) curl to `localhost` burns ~200ms/request on IPv6 ::1→IPv4 fallback (server listens 0.0.0.0 only) — ALWAYS curl 127.0.0.1; (2) anything measured while `/api/data/cached-days` ran queued behind a 3.2-SECOND synchronous better-sqlite3 call. Bench the handler's pieces standalone (read-only second connection on the WAL DB works fine) before touching code.
- **THE server blocker — cached-days:** window-function SQL (PARTITION BY `DATE(datetime(ts,'unixepoch'))` per row over ~700k 5/15/60 rows) = 3.2s sync stall on every fresh page load and every data_updated invalidation. Replaced with `server/day-cache.ts`: integer-day GROUP BY cold build (~1.2s, prewarmed at boot in index.ts) + rowid-watermark incremental refresh + always re-agg last/today day (forming-bucket upserts UPDATE in place — no new rowid) + `invalidateDayCache` hooked CENTRALLY in `broadcast()` on data_updated (every bulk-rewrite flow that tells clients to refetch passes through it). Output verified byte-identical vs the old SQL on all 4 symbols. **3,200ms → 6–9ms warm.** GOTCHA: `id > ?` needs `NOT INDEXED` — the planner otherwise picks the (symbol,resolution,timestamp) autoindex and scans 700k rows (75ms) instead of the INTEGER-PRIMARY-KEY range (0.4ms).
- **Fast ET clock (`etWallClock` in shared/firing/session.ts):** per-UTC-HOUR offset cache — America/New_York DST transitions always land exactly on whole UTC hours, so the offset is constant within any hour bucket; Intl computes each bucket ONCE (never hardcode offsets, per CLAUDE.md). isRTH/isMarketBreak/isAfter315ET/isWeekendClosed rewritten on it; equivalence proven on 111,276 timestamps incl. 30s-dense DST-transition sweeps (0 mismatches, 26× faster). validateSignalRow read filter ~40ms → ~2ms per 2.6k rows. GOTCHA: formatter CONSTRUCTION was already hoisted everywhere — the per-CALL formatToParts cost was the remaining eater; worse, client `fmtEtTime` constructed a formatter PER SIGNAL ROW (~seconds of main thread per 1m refetch) — now one module-level formatter.
- **Client refetch storm killed:** `signal_new` broadcasts now CARRY the upserted rows (grouped per symbol|interval; iPhone ignores `rows` and refetches as before). useTerminalData applies them incrementally (same filters + mirrors the upsert's COALESCE guards: null label/signalType/exit fields keep displayed values), `signal_removed` deletes by natural key, safety poll 30s → 120s, and fetches pass `?since=<cutoff>` (server-side gte; 1m response 1.96MB → 583KB @ 24ms). Verified in-tab: 3 total signals fetches in 15 min (was: fetch on every fire + 30s poll, ~2MB each on 1m).
- **Hidden-engine long-task storm (the browser side of "the app is slow"):** longtask probe on "/" measured p50 **2,294ms** tasks at 13/min (~46% main-thread busy) — the four `bgSignalsXm` memos keyed `[windowedCandles, ...]` re-ran FOUR full runFactEngine passes on every 2s forming-bar update even though the three non-viewed intervals consume STABLE bgXmData arrays. Keyed each on its own candle source (viewed interval still keys on windowedCandles; use a module-level EMPTY const — a `?? []` literal in a dep expression mints a new identity per render and defeats the memo). Also widened the forming-bar throttle 2s → 5s (bar-close messages bypass it — bar-close signal latency unchanged; only C8 intra-candle cadence widened). After: p50 310ms / single main pass 150–400ms per update. NOTE: runFactEngine treats EVERY candle as closed (no forming-bar skip) — freezing closed-bar recomputes behind a signature WOULD change live intra-bar fire/retract semantics; a deeper fix needs engine-side incremental evaluation, not memo tricks.
- **Residual observations (reported, not fixed):** `/api/live/bar` is polled 15–25×/s across ~5 connected clients + iPhone (0–2ms in-memory handler — log noise, not a lag driver; candidate for riding the WS tick channel). Server startup (yahoo backfill + MW scan) still queues early requests for ~30–60s after boot.

**2026-07-29 — COMBO-LEVEL QUALITY GATE: only historically winning fact-COMBINATIONS fire (comboKeyOf/comboGateAllows) + full regen under the rolled window**
- **The model:** a signal's COUNTED fact families (Vec/YB/ICT/Fr/FG/FP/Zone via `FACT_FAMILY`) form a canonical sorted combo key (`comboKeyOf` — lives IN the generated quality-gate.ts so engine + harness share one helper; `backtestable:false` PML/TML facts are excluded so live-only evidence can't move a signal into an unjudged combo and bypass a blocked base combo). Engine consult (`comboGateAllows`) sits AFTER the class gate, BEFORE the cooldown cursor, in BOTH runFactEngine and evaluateFormingBar; lookup = `combo@interval` (verdict needs n≥20) → all-interval `combo` (n≥15) → absent = allowed. CRITICAL DESIGN POINT: a blocked combo suppresses EVEN ≥3-counted-fact signals — the multi-fact override bypasses the CLASS gate only (Fr+ICT+Vec+YB was the motivating 4-fact loser). Exempt by signalType: zone-reaction + ETH vector-side-entry (structural zero-data rules).
- **Derivation (recursion-guarded):** inside computeGateDataWalkForward, combo verdicts are judged on the HELD-OUT half's rows that the freshly derived CLASS verdicts would admit live (counted≥3 OR class allowed/absent), simulated under the SAME first-half class exits — rows come from the NEUTRAL ungated pass, never from a pass that consulted any combo verdict (the 2026-07-14 feedback-loop lesson applied). Thin combos carry forward the previous file's verdict lowConfidence, like classes. MC exits then calibrate on the combo-gated PHASE-C population (the actual live trade population).
- **THE WINDOW ROLL INVERTED THE CLASS GATE — not the combo change:** held-out half is now 2026-06-06..07-29 and contains the genuinely bad Jul 20–29 (Yahoo-provisional) stretch: ALL fact-engine classes flipped BLOCK (fe@1m 0.959/−0.328 n=1179, fe@5m 0.927/−0.604, fe@15m 0.868/−0.895, fe@60m 0.567) and vector-side-entry@1m flipped PASS (1.098/+0.987 n=1041). Final set = vse@1m 2,153 + fact-engine 568 (all ≥3-fact overrides ∩ combo-allowed — the combo gate is now the PRIMARY filter on confluence trades). **2026-07-15 — FRACTAL EXCHANGE GUIDE STUDY → fractal-geometry confirmation facts + E/S vector crack + PML/TML live module + 3-month recalibration**
- **E/S VECTOR CRACKED EMPIRICALLY, NOT FROM THE GUIDES** (no guide contains any formula — the Vector guide explicitly calls the line non-replicable): mined 24 "E VECTOR"/"S VECTOR" comment-label pairs out of `data/reference/*.mwml` (labels placed 15:57–16:17 ET) and fit candidates against MES bars. **E = prior trading day's 17:00 ET futures close (MAE 2.18 pts, max 5.63, n=14 same-contract days); S ≈ prior day's 16:00 ET close (cash-close proxy, MAE 4.97)** — the E−S spread tracks the prior day's 16:00→17:00 move (sign 10/12, one +49-pt day matched by +53). RULE: a new corroborator family must prove itself against the BEFORE pass before its kinds may count.
- **decide() geometry to remember when designing contra tests**: every minimal qualifying stack weighs ≥3 (strong zone 3; zone 2+fp 1; SE 3) and CONTRADICTION_MARGIN is 2, so a weight-1 contra NEVER suppresses alone (3−1=2 still fires) — chase/exhaustion at default weight only bite alongside chop or opposing facts (deliberate + documented). Test fixtures for contra rules must (a) disable the quality gate (the 3-mo gate blocks fact-engine@5m at n=2 counted — three separate fixture failures traced to this), (b) respect the recent-bars windows (a reclaim 3 bars back still counts — spacer bars), (c) clear the 15:15 close cutoff AND the HOD-proximity window, (d) know that a NORMAL solo zone reaction never qualifies (strong-only solo).
- **PML/TML = net GAMMA exposure profile, not settlement payout**: the guide's "wings balance to zero" + interior negative peak + both signs is the GEX signature — max-pain-style payout curves are edge-maximal and never decay (first implementation was degenerate at the ±8% window edge, observed live). Final model: Σcalls OI·Γ − Σputs OI·Γ (flat σ=0.15, T from nearest expiry), PML = interior peak-negative, TML = zero-CROSSING nearest spot (the guide's "washout zone" — plain argmin|exposure| degenerates to the wing edge). Live-only by construction: `backtestable:false` facts + live-edge-only enumeration (`closeTime ≥ nowSec − 2·barSec`) + the harness never supplies `liveLevels` — three independent guards, zero backtest footprint (verified: 0 moneyline traces in 2,805 rows).

- **The "hours-long workbook grind" was an INFINITE LOOP, not slow exceljs**: the combo-sheet name deduper `while (usedNames.has(name)) name = name.slice(0, 29) + " 2"` reproduces the EXISTING " 2" name verbatim on the THIRD collision of one truncated 31-char prefix — adding "Fractal Geometry" to combo display names guaranteed triple collisions and the process spun for 6.7 CPU-hours (the prior agent's final run left a stale 16:06 xlsx for the same reason). Fixed with an incrementing counter (`base + " " + k++`); with per-sheet progress logs added, the whole 39-sheet workbook builds + writes in **2 seconds**. RULES: any collision-resolution loop must PROVABLY make progress (a counter, not a constant suffix); instrument multi-minute phases with per-step logs BEFORE trusting "it's just slow"; a pegged core with frozen log output for >10 min is a loop until proven otherwise. Also: `tsx` children survive TaskStop of the parent shell (kill the child PID via `taskkill //PID <id> //F` in git-bash — single slashes are mangled to a path).

- **Default-ON corroborators broke 14 existing tests two ways** (both fixed by pinning `ictEnabled:false, fractalEnabled:false` on mechanics fixtures, mirroring the qualityGateEnabled convention): (1) trending-FCO facts attach to almost any strong fixture bar, turning solo zone-reactions into 2-fact `fact-engine` signals — which flips their CLASS and hands the outcome to the regenerated gate data (D13 cooldown fixture broke only AFTER the gate regen blocked fact-engine@5m); (2) exact-fact-count assertions (`counted === 3`) drift when a real corroborator joins.
  - Action: any fixture asserting counts/types/cooldown MUST pin every default-ON feature OFF unless it is the feature under test; a fixture can pass against today's generated gate and fail against tomorrow's.
  - Confidence: high
- **3-MONTH REPORTING WINDOW (standing user directive):** all backtests now cover trades ENTERED in the last ~3 months (WINDOW_START_KEY, currently 2026-04-15); candles load from LOOKBACK_START_KEY (Jan-1) for the 50-day yellowbox walk-forward + warmup. Gate verdicts resting on n<50 window trades CARRY FORWARD the previous full-history verdict with `lowConfidence:true` + note (interface fields added to BOTH the checked-in quality-gate.ts AND the template inside writeQualityGateFile — they must move together); the gate-consistency test exempts lowConfidence entries. The BEFORE pass (apples-to-apples) runs the OLD engine config from an `OLD_GATE` deep-copy snapshotted at module load — same pattern as the neutral-baseline lesson: any comparison against "what was live" must pin the PREVIOUS generated data explicitly.
  - Confidence: high
- **Results (2026-04-15..2026-07-15, MES):** old engine 323 trades / +1.51 exp / PF 1.31 / +487 pts → new engine 2,763 trades / +1.46 exp / PF 1.23 / +4,030 pts. 2,560 newly fired (2,558 carrying ICT/fractal facts — mostly yellowbox-break + corroborator and side-entry + corroborator RTH confluences that previously died as 1-fact), 120 old fires gone, 100 chop-suppressed, 95.8% of final trades carry a confirmation fact. Window gate: fact-engine@1m the only rule-allowed class (PF 1.05/+0.16); 5m/15m/60m confluence + all vse classes blocked (60m/15m trades in the final set are ≥3-fact overrides — final calibrated stats healthy: 15m PF 1.16, 60m PF 1.19). CAVEAT reported to user: the ≥3-fact override pool measured PF 0.97/-0.10 UNGATED at provisional exits (n=1,122 — corroborators made 3-fact setups ~14x more common than the old n=82 pool); with calibrated exits the override classes measure positive, but the override now carries real volume and deserves its own gate review next run.

Same shared computeCoreZones formula → identical results, but wasted compute and a literal that rots on every bump. (Background-task chip filed.)
  - Action: scripts reading a versioned cache must reference the version CONSTANT, never a literal.
